# ShoppyDrive Audit Remediation Tracker

Operational source of truth for remediation status. The original findings are defined by two reports: `docs/SHAPPYDRIVE_FORENSIC_AUDIT_REPORT.txt` (CRIT/HIGH/MED/LOW IDs, 2026-11-01) and `docs/SHOPPYDRIVE_FULL_SYSTEM_FORENSIC_AUDIT_REPORT_2026-11-02.md` (FSA-01–FSA-24). `docs/MASTER_FIXING_TRACKER.md` remains the record of earlier remediation work (PROMO-LOY-001, PAY-RETURN-001, …).

**Last updated:** 2026-11-03. **Git baseline:** `origin/main` = `17a1501`, level with local `main`. The push of 2026-11-03 moved it from `0ef9d9a` to `17a1501` with `8d17320` (FSA-13), `8cb8ff7` (FSA-09), `1d16a71` (FSA-11), `9c840f7` (FSA-03, FSA-12), `985d73b` (FSA-01, FSA-04, FSA-05), `d1f258c` (tracker documentation) and `17a1501` (handoff documentation). One stash (`stash@{0}`, untouched); no tags; no other branches. Pushing `main` deploys to Hostinger and runs `migrate --force`.

**Deployment record (2026-11-03, push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`):**

- **Verified from the workflow and logs:** "Deploy to Hostinger" run `41575932713` succeeded, including `Required checks (Pint + Pest)` and `Build & Deploy`. `migrate --force` printed "Nothing to migrate", so no migration ran in this deploy. The runtime check reported `APP_ENV=production` with `APP_DEBUG=false` before and after the sync. The health check passed on attempt 1.
- **Verified by smoke check:** `https://shoppydrive.com/`, `/up` and `/products` returned 200. `/` returned the ShoppyDrive page with Inertia page data. `/order/SMOKE-NOT-REAL/confirmation` returned 404. This is availability only: no order, payment, login or admin action was exercised.
- **Non-gating workflows that failed on `17a1501`:** `Run Tests` failed only on `BuyNowCheckoutTest` ("buy now places an order without ever creating a cart row"). The test buys 2 units of a product with random factory stock from 0 to 500, so it fails when the stock is 0 or 1; the same suite passed in the deploy's required checks. `Lint` failed on Prettier in `hero-slides/create.tsx`, `hero-slides/edit.tsx` and `Home.tsx`, none of which the seven commits changed. Neither is fixed yet.
- **Production database (owner-supplied pre-check through Laravel's PDO connection):** MariaDB 11.8.9, not MySQL; `READ-COMMITTED`; `innodb_snapshot_isolation` ON; 0 invalid `pending_checkouts.checkout_data` rows; 74 tables (73 expected; the extra table is not yet identified).
- **Pending functional verification:** FSA-05 product visibility impact, the expected unique indexes, `job_batches`, and COD, mobile-banking and Google-login smoke tests. The read-only production checks had not been run when this deploy happened. No finding below is `VERIFIED_IN_PRODUCTION` on this evidence.

**Deployment evidence (2026-11-03, read-only `gh run list --workflow=deploy.yml`):** "Deploy to Hostinger" succeeded for `1db8482` (2026-11-02 08:24 UTC, first push containing `18125d4` and `3254bac`), `84aa1c0` and `0ef9d9a` (10:27 UTC). The run logs did not show `migrate` output, so which migrations ran and with what result (including whether MED-06 skipped an index) is **not verified**. Code on `main` is therefore `DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED`, never `VERIFIED_IN_PRODUCTION`, until production evidence exists.

**Statuses (from 2026-11-03):** OPEN, IN_PROGRESS, FIXED_LOCALLY, TESTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, VERIFIED_IN_PRODUCTION, BLOCKED, PARTIALLY_FIXED, ACCEPTED_RISK (plus NOT_APPLICABLE for LOW-07, kept as recorded). Never mark progress without evidence. Old statuses in the historical sections below are kept verbatim; the table below supersedes them. Historical vocabulary (pre-2026-11-03): NOT_STARTED, INVESTIGATING, IN_PROGRESS, BLOCKED, PARTIALLY_COMPLETED, COMPLETED, ACCEPTED_RISK, NOT_APPLICABLE; `COMPLETED (LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION)` meant verified locally and not deployed at the time. Completed findings are not reopened without new evidence.

| FSA-11 | Medium | Admin can cancel a shipped order | Fix DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED (`1d16a71`); visibility follow-up + **owner-approved skip-restock mechanism** IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED (2026-11-03) | Admin cancel refuses `shipped`; admin order page flags an unrecognized courier report for manual investigation. **New (owner-approved):** a mother-admin-only `confirm-lost` action (`OrderLostParcelService`) records a confirmed-lost determination — requires existing courier evidence (an `OrderDeliveryVerification` with outcome `not_delivered` for every linked consignment, reusing `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility()` exactly as refunds already do), is idempotent, and sets a new `orders.lost_confirmed_at` column that makes `InventoryService::restoreForOrder()` skip restocking the next time this one order is cancelled or refunded — every other order's cancel/refund restocking is completely unaffected. | SEC-021 | Medium | Back button after logout shows account data | IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (`876640e`, follow-up fix `7addfdf`), NOT PUSHED (2026-11-05) | Two parts: (1) `routes/account.php` and `routes/admin.php`'s authenticated groups now carry a `history.encrypt` middleware (Inertia's built-in history encryption, gated — see follow-up below). (2) A new `App\Http\Responses\LogoutResponse` (bound in `FortifyServiceProvider`, same pattern as the existing `LoginResponse`/`RegisterResponse`) calls `Inertia::clearHistory()` for customer logout; `AdminAuthController::logout()` calls it directly. Both deliberately call it *after* `session()->invalidate()` — that flushes all session attributes, including the `clearHistory` flag itself if set any earlier (e.g. a naive `Logout` event listener, which fires *before* `invalidate()` — tried first, found broken, reverted). **Follow-up fix (`7addfdf`, owner-reported):** the original cut used inertia-laravel's bare `inertia.encrypt` middleware unconditionally — history encryption needs `window.crypto.subtle`, which doesn't exist without HTTPS (or localhost), and Inertia's client throws ("Unable to encrypt history") rather than skipping quietly, as an uncaught promise rejection that stops the page visit from completing. Every `/admin` and `/account` page visited over plain HTTP (any local/LAN dev visit) got stuck "loading" and never rendered — reported against the admin flash-sales page specifically, but not specific to it. New `ConditionallyEncryptHistory` middleware (alias `history.encrypt`) only calls `Inertia::encryptHistory()` when `$request->secure()`; production is unaffected, since `RedirectToHttps` already forces HTTPS there first. See SEC-021 notes | `HistoryEncryptionAfterLogoutTest` (6 tests: the original 3 plus the follow-up's regression tests — encryption off over plain HTTP for both guards, on over an actual `https://` URL); full auth/Inertia sweep (953 tests) + full suite green; Pint clean | Functional/production verification (real browser back-button check); **not verified in a live browser this session** — no browser automation tool was available | Not deployed | None |

Beyond the obvious (admin customer page's raw `->load('socialAccounts')`), the subtler path: `Settings\ConnectionsController::edit()` lazy-loads this relation onto the *same* User instance `HandleInertiaRequests`'s shared `auth.user` prop reads on every page, so the loaded, unhidden models would have leaked there too, not just in that page's own (already-safe) `connections` prop. See SEC-004 notes | New `Admin/CustomerShowTest.php` and a new test in `Settings/ConnectionsTest.php`: seed a real token value, assert the whole response body (not just the page's own prop) never contains it; full social-account sweep (50 tests) + full suite green | None open | Not deployed | None |
| SEC-005 | Medium | Changing an admin password does not log out other sessions | IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED (2026-11-05) | Laravel's built-in `auth.session` middleware (`AuthenticateSession`) only ever checks the app's *default* guard (hardcoded `getDefaultDriver()`, no guard parameter) — adding it to the admin route group would silently do nothing, since the default guard here is `web`. New `AuthenticateAdminSession` middleware (alias `admin.session`, added to `routes/admin.php`'s authenticated group) mirrors the same password-hash-fingerprint approach for `admin` specifically, keyed by **admin id + hash** (not hash alone — a session switching to a *different* admin, which `Auth::login()` itself doesn't guard against, and which is exactly what `actingAs()` does between two test calls, is a new identity to adopt, not a stolen session to punish). `AdminController::update()` rotates `remember_token` on any password change and resyncs the *current* session's own fingerprint on a self-edit, so changing your own password doesn't also log out the session that changed it. See SEC-005 notes | New `Admin/AdminSessionSecurityTest.php` (5 tests: stays valid normally; signed out when the password changes elsewhere; self-change resyncs without self-logout; switching identity is adopted, not punished; fingerprint is id+hash, not hash alone). One pre-existing test (`AdminAuthorizationTest`) split in two — it switched `actingAs()` identity mid-test sharing one session, an artifact the id+hash design otherwise handles, but splitting was still the more realistic shape. Full Admin suite (811 tests) + full suite green | Functional/production verification | Not deployed | None |
| SEC-010 | Medium | Weaker password policy and no per-account login limit for admins | IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED (2026-11-05) | Four sub-fixes: (1) `AdminController`'s two `Password::min(8)` calls and `CreateMotherAdminCommand`'s one now use `Password::default()` — the same environment-aware policy (12+ chars, mixed case, symbols, breach-checked in production) customers already get via `AppServiceProvider::configureDefaults()`'s single shared factory; behaviorally identical to before in non-production, since `Password::default()` itself falls back to `min(8)` whenever that factory returns null. (2) Admin self-password-change now requires `current_password:admin`, matching the customer-facing `PasswordUpdateRequest`; resetting a *different* admin's password (the mother's privileged action) is unaffected. (3) Added a third, email-only (any IP) rate limit to the `admin-login` limiter — the existing email+IP and IP-only limits leave one account open to a slow attack spread thinly across many source IPs. (4) A failed admin login is now logged (`Log::warning`, email + IP, never the password). See SEC-010 notes | New `Admin/AdminPasswordPolicyTest.php` (5 tests) and 2 new tests in `AdminLoginThrottleTest.php` (spread-IP throttling; failure logging via `Log::spy()`); full Admin/Auth/Settings sweep (938 tests) + full suite green | Functional/production verification (the production-only password policy branch is, by its nature, unobservable from the testing environment) | Not deployed | None |
| FE-005 | Medium | A server error empties a logged-in customer's cart | IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED (2026-11-05) | Root cause confirmed: `AppServiceProvider::configureErrorPages()` calls `Inertia::flushShared()` for every 5xx response, so `auth` isn't `{user: null}` on an error page, it's *absent from props entirely*. `resources/js/app.tsx`'s navigate handler read `props.auth?.user?.id ?? null`, which can't tell "the server confirmed you're a guest" apart from "the server told us nothing" — a 500 on any page looked exactly like a real logout and wiped the cart/wishlist via `applyLogout()`. Fixed by checking `'auth' in props` before using its absence to decide anything, short-circuiting the whole block (cart hydration, bundle-recipe reset, wishlist-merge-snapshot too) on an error page instead of just the logout branch. See FE-005 notes | `ErrorPagesTest.php` already proved `settings` is dropped on 500 — added `.missing('auth')` to the same assertion to document the exact server-side contract the fix depends on. No JS test runner in this project (eslint/tsc/`npm run build` clean); **not verified in a live browser this session** — no browser automation tool was available | Manual browser verification (log in with an item in the cart, visit a page that throws a 500, confirm the cart survives) | Not deployed | None |

The rest of the finding (captcha/Turnstile, a per-product pending-COD cap, phone OTP above a threshold) is explicitly marked "Business choice" by the audit itself and was deliberately not added — it needs a product decision (which challenge provider, what threshold, what UX cost to real customers) and likely a new dependency, not a code-only fix. See BIZ-011 notes | New test in `CheckoutOrderPlacementTest.php`: quantity 3000000000 now fails validation (`items.0.quantity`) instead of reaching the database; no order created. Full Cart/Checkout sweep (181 tests) green, Pint clean | Owner decision needed on anti-abuse controls (captcha provider, pending-COD cap, OTP threshold) before further work here | Not deployed | Business choice — see notes |
| BIZ-010 | Medium | Loyalty points could be spent twice through a late mobile payment (FSA-07 regression) | IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (`3a38443`), NOT PUSHED | `PendingCheckout::scopeHoldingPointsFor()` stops counting a reservation the moment it expires, by design (so an abandoned checkout doesn't lock a customer's points forever) — but `MobilePaymentController`'s FSA-07 grace can still confirm that same checkout late if proof-of-payment exists, debiting points via `LoyaltyPointsService::debitPointsForOrder()` that may have already been legitimately redeemed again elsewhere in the gap. `debitPointsForOrder()` now checks the locked user's current balance before debiting; if insufficient, it skips the debit (never goes negative), logs a warning, and leaves an order-status-history note for manual review — the order itself still completes since the customer already paid the (already-discounted) amount. Chose this over extending the hold ("keep it alive when proof exists") because that would need matching an unknown future TrxID against a live SMS feed per pending checkout — a materially bigger change for a bounded, real-payment-gated edge case. See BIZ-010 notes | New test in `MobilePaymentMatchingTest.php` mirrors the audit's A17 repro (redeem 100 points via mobile banking, let the hold lapse, spend the same 100 on a COD order, then submit the late TrxID) — balance stays 0, not negative, and the late order's history records the skipped debit. Full Loyalty/Mobile/Checkout/Referral sweep (233 tests) green, Pint clean | Functional/production verification | Not deployed | None |
| BIZ-008 | Medium | Refunded orders stayed "paid" and kept counting as revenue | IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED (`51c797b`), NOT PUSHED | Two root causes. (1) `RefundService::record()` compared cumulative refunds against the raw collected amount (shipping included) to decide "fully refunded" — since the refundable cap deliberately excludes shipping, a full refund could never reach that bar, so `payment_status` stayed PAID forever. Now compares against `RefundCalculator::capPaisa()` instead. (2) `DashboardController`'s revenue queries counted an order's full `total` even once it was partially refunded (still correctly PAID). New `RefundCalculator::refundedAmountsFor()` (kept inside the refund-ledger access allow-list `CourierDeliveryEvidenceArchTest` enforces) batches `order_refunds` sums per order; `DashboardController::netRevenue()`/the 7-day chart now subtract them. See BIZ-008 notes | Two pre-existing tests (`OrderRefundTest.php`, `MarkOrderRefundedTest.php`) asserted the old bug as intended behavior ("960 collected, 60 shipping kept" staying PAID) — updated to expect REFUNDED, since a full refund of the product cap is in fact a full refund. New `DashboardRevenueTest.php` case: a ৳1000 order with a ৳300 ledger refund shows ৳700 revenue, not ৳1000. Full Refund/Dashboard/Loyalty/Courier/Return sweep (428 tests) + full suite (2080 tests) green, Pint clean | Functional/production verification | Not deployed | None |

**Accepted risks preserved (not findings):** ADMIN-2FA-001 (declined, final), DEPLOY-SAFETY-001 C1 (no pre-deploy backup), M1 (`StrictHostKeyChecking=no`), 17 moderate npm advisories, XSS-SINK-GAP-001 (`Page.content`), MED-19, LOW-01, LOW-02, LOW-09.

**Open from earlier work:** DEPLOY-SAFETY-001 Tier 2, `nunomaduro/pao` rename, E2E-COVERAGE-001, CI-MYSQL-001, TEST-FLAKE-001, OPS-001.

**Priority order (2026-11-03):** FSA-01 → FSA-02 → FSA-03 + FSA-04 → FSA-05 → FSA-06 → FSA-07 + FSA-08 → FSA-09, FSA-10, FSA-11 → remaining FSA, LOW and earlier unresolved items.

## BIZ-016 notes

Fixed as part of the same-session flash-sale consistency sweep (6a37ddc), before the P1 resume list below was drawn up — recorded here now so the row/notes exist for this finding like every other.

## SEC-007 notes

`readEnvValue()` still reads the token from `.env` directly (unchanged, pre-existing); the fix only gates whether it's sent to the browser at all, based on the authenticated admin's own `sms_forwarder` `update` permission via `AdminSections`. Regenerate stays gated by the existing `AdminSections::middleware('sms_forwarder', 'update')` route middleware — unchanged, already correct.

## BE-009 notes

Deliberately did not touch `config/app.php`'s `UTC` timezone — the finding's own remediation note says to keep stored values in UTC and fix display boundaries only, and changing the app timezone globally would ripple into every `created_at`/log timestamp, cache TTL, and scheduled-job boundary in the app (`rewards:grant-due` already runs on an explicit `Asia/Dhaka` schedule per `routes/console.php`, independent of `app.timezone`). `DailyVisit.date` is a plain `DATE` column (timezone-agnostic once stored), so fixing what string `TrackDailyVisit` writes to it is sufficient going forward; existing rows written before this fix keep their old (UTC-day) boundary, which is an acceptable, non-destructive gap for a display-only fix.

## BIZ-011 notes

100 was chosen to match `LandingPageController`'s existing `max:100` on its single-product quantity field — no new business rule invented, just applied consistently to the three request classes the audit flagged as missing it. Did not touch `AppServiceProvider.php:192-207`'s rate limits (10/10min per IP, 5/hour per phone) — those are an existing, working, deliberate business choice already, not a bug; the audit's own severity note frames tightening them (plus adding a bot challenge) as a separate product decision.

## BIZ-010 notes

The order-status-history note ("Review manually") is the only admin-visible surface for this today — there is no dedicated flagged-orders queue. Given the exploit requires a real completed payment (bounded loss, matches the finding's own severity note) and is rare, a plain history note plus the `Log::warning` was judged sufficient rather than building a new review workflow.

## BIZ-007 notes

Did not add a Steadfast SHIPPED mapping to `CourierOrderStatusMapper` or start writing `Order.shipped_at` in this pass — that's a materially bigger change (affects `ShippedWithTrackingMail`, `CourierEvidenceChain`'s refund-eligibility reads of `shipped_at`, and SSR/email assumptions elsewhere) that deserves its own review rather than being rushed in alongside the narrower cancel-guard fix. The consignment-ID check closes the actual exploitable gap (cash collected + stock restored on an order still at the courier) without touching that wider surface.

## BIZ-008 notes

`RefundCalculator::refundedAmountsFor()` was added there (not read directly from `DashboardController`) because `CourierDeliveryEvidenceArchTest`'s "the refund ledger is written only by the refund service" rule confines `OrderRefund` usage to an explicit allow-list (`RefundService`, `RefundCalculator`, `LoyaltyPointsService`, `Order`) — reading it straight from the dashboard failed that arch test immediately, confirming the rule actually guards against ad-hoc ledger access, not just writes.

## BIZ-005 notes

`app/Services/RefundService.php::assertRefundable()` previously rejected any `OrderStatus::CANCELLED` order outright, before even checking payment status. A paid order could still end up cancelled via the customer self-cancel route (`routes/account.php`) or `CourierOrderStatusMapper`'s return/cancel report — neither checks `payment_status` — leaving that money with no ledger path back to the customer. Fix: the cancelled-rejection now only fires when the order was also never paid; a cancelled-and-paid order falls through to the normal paid/courier-evidence checks and can be refunded. Verified the `CANCELLED→REFUNDED` transition's `Order::booted()` side effects (stock restore, coupon release, loyalty/referral reversal) are all idempotent (`stock_restored_at`, `coupon_released_at`, `points_refunded_at`, `referral_discount_used_order_id` guards) before relying on firing them twice being safe. FSA-06 (admin can't directly cancel a paid order) is a separate, unchanged, still-approved policy — it exists to force "Mark as Refunded" discipline for admin actions specifically, not to make cancelled-and-paid unrefundable in general.

## BIZ-003 notes

Root cause: `Checkout.tsx` kept two disconnected coupon states — the cart store's `coupon` (set by Cart.tsx's apply/remove) and the page's own local `appliedCoupon` (its own independent `useState(null)`, never seeded from the store). A generic "Discount" row read the store's discount for display; a separate "Coupon (CODE)" row read the local state; only the local state's code was ever submitted. A guest who applied a coupon on `/cart` saw the discounted total at checkout (from the store) but was charged full price (the local state, and thus the submission, never had it). A logged-in customer re-applying at checkout got both rows simultaneously — double-subtracted. Fix: `appliedCoupon` now seeds from the store on mount (`buyNowItem` excluded, matching the old `discount = buyNowItem ? 0 : …` exclusion) and every apply/remove syncs back to the store; the redundant "Discount" row/variable was removed entirely. No PHP changed. **Not verified in a live browser this session** — no browser automation tool was available; recommend a manual check (apply a coupon as guest on `/cart`, confirm `/checkout` shows one discount line matching the charge) before relying on this fully.

## BIZ-001 notes

Root cause: `CheckoutController::store()` called `syncUserCart()`/`syncSessionCart()` (→ `CartManager::replaceItems()`) *before* `revalidatePrices()`. `replaceItems()` always overwrites every item's stored price to the current resolved price, regardless of what the client submits (items never even included a price field before this fix) — so by the time `revalidatePrices()` ran, it was comparing the current price against itself and could never detect a change. A product price edited (or a flash sale starting/ending) between checkout page load and submission was silently charged at the new price with no warning. Fix: `Checkout.tsx` now submits each line's displayed `price`; `PlaceOrderRequest` validates it (`nullable|numeric|min:0` — optional, so every other order-creation path that never sends it is unaffected); new `CartManager::submittedPricesStale()` compares it against the live resolved price *before* the sync runs, refusing with the same "Some prices in your cart have changed" message if they disagree. The submitted price is never used to decide the charge — only whether to refuse before anything is written; `resolvePrice()` still decides the actual charge, exactly as before. Rewrote `FlashSalePricingTest`'s `'a client-submitted price field is ignored'` test: previously a mismatched client price was silently dropped and the order still succeeded at the correct price; now it's refused outright (safer — the scenario is indistinguishable from legitimate staleness), so the test now asserts refusal instead of silent correction, plus a new test for the stale-then-retry-at-corrected-price flow.

## BIZ-002 notes

Root cause: `Checkout.tsx`'s submitted items never included `bundle_id`/`bundle_unit_quantity` at all, even though `PlaceOrderRequest`, `CartManager::replaceItems()`/`normalizePayloads()`, and `BundleService` already fully supported both (confirmed via the pre-existing `tests/Feature/BundleCheckoutTest.php`, which passed against the backend alone before any frontend change). Sync stripped the bundle grouping from every submission, `BundleService::reconcile()`/`autoAssemble()` re-detected "a price changed" on every attempt, and the customer could never get past "Some prices have changed." Fix: added both fields to the submitted payload, the same `items.map()` BIZ-001 also touches. Added one new test combining both fixes' fields (`price` + `bundle_id` + `bundle_unit_quantity` together) to prove they don't interact badly — a bundled line's submitted price must equal the *prorated bundle price*, not the plain product price, or BIZ-001's stale-price check would incorrectly refuse a correct bundle checkout.

## BIZ-006 notes

Root cause: nothing checked stock before staging a `PendingCheckout` for mobile banking (cart checkout's `CheckoutController::initiateMobileBanking()`, or the landing-page equivalent in `LandingPageController::placeOrder()`) — `InventoryService::decrementForOrderItem()` only ever runs once the customer has already paid and submitted their TrxID (`MobilePaymentController::submit()` / `LandingPageController::submitPayment()`). An out-of-stock item still showed bKash/Nagad payment instructions; money was taken with no way to fulfill the order. Fix: new `InventoryService::assertAvailable()` — deliberately read-only and unlocked (no order/transaction exists yet to lock within) — mirrors `decrementForOrderItem()`'s ordinary-stock and flash-sale-cap checks without writing anything, called right before each of the two `PendingCheckout::create()` call sites. This can't close the race entirely (stock can still run out between this check and actual payment minutes later) — `decrementForOrderItem()` remains the one authoritative, locked check at real order creation — but it closes the common, avoidable case the finding describes. The audit's secondary suggestions ("share the grace logic" between cart and landing-page flows, "add a pending-checkout to the SMS reconciliation screen") were not done — out of scope for this pass, the core "check stock before showing payment instructions" fix was prioritized.

## BIZ-004 notes

Root cause: `Admin\IncompleteOrderController::placeOrder()` created `OrderItem`s directly, with no call to `InventoryService` at all — the one order-creation path (COD, ZiniPay, mobile banking, landing page, admin manual order all call `decrementForOrderItem()`; this didn't). Stock was never actually taken, yet later cancelling the resulting order still ran `InventoryService::restoreForOrder()` via `Order::booted()`, which restocked quantities that had never been decremented — inflating stock above its pre-conversion count. Fix: added `InventoryService $inventory` as a method-injected dependency and call `decrementForOrderItem()` per item inside the existing transaction, wrapped in the same `try`/`catch (InsufficientStockException)` → `back()->withErrors(['items' => …])` pattern `Admin\OrderController`'s manual-order flow already uses. Also added `VariantBelongsToProduct` to `ConvertIncompleteOrderRequest`'s `items.*.variant_id` rule (previously missing — the audit's "validate the variant owner" note; this class of bug is already closed everywhere else, e.g. FSA-01). Existing tests in `tests/Feature/Admin/IncompleteOrderManagementTest.php` relied on `ProductFactory`'s default `stock_quantity` (`fake()->numberBetween(0, 500)`) — harmless when stock was never checked, but a ~0.4%-per-test flake risk now that it is — so every `Product::factory()->create()` call in that file now sets an explicit, sufficient `stock_quantity`.

## BIZ-013 notes

Root cause: `CheckoutController` refuses with `withErrors(['coupon_code' => …])` or `withErrors(['order_token' => …])` in three places, none paired with the `error` session flash. Checkout.tsx's `flash?.error` banner is the only thing on this page that renders a refusal that isn't tied to a named shipping-address input — `form.setError()` sets the Inertia errors bag, but nothing reads `errors.coupon_code`/`errors.order_token` for display (the same gap BIZ-001/BIZ-006 already fixed for `items`). A coupon that expired/was deactivated between being applied and Place Order was silently rejected: the submit button just reset with the stale discount still showing. Fix: added the same `.with('error', $message)` to all three refusals. Also: Checkout.tsx's `onError` handler now calls `applyCoupon(null)` (the BIZ-003 sync wrapper) when `coupon_code` is the rejected key, so the UI stops showing a discount the server just refused. The `order_token` lock-timeout path (two near-simultaneous submissions of the same token) has no automated test — it needs a second process genuinely holding the `Cache::lock()`, not practically simulable in a single-process test run.

## SEC-021 notes

Root cause: Inertia keeps every visited page's data in `window.history.state` for its own back/forward handling — normally invisible to the user, but on a shared PC, pressing Back after a customer or admin logs out re-shows that stored state (orders, addresses, whatever the page last rendered) with no server round-trip, since there's nothing server-side to intercept a client-side history pop. Confirmed in the audit's own browser evidence. Inertia's own answer is history encryption, which has two independent pieces, both previously missing: (1) opt in to encryption for the sensitive route groups — otherwise the stored state is plain JSON regardless of anything else — and (2) rotate the encryption key on logout, so even a page visited *during* the session that's about to end becomes unreadable once it's over, not just future ones. Implementation note: the first attempt wired (2) via an `Illuminate\Auth\Events\Logout` listener in `AppServiceProvider` — wrong, because that event fires *during* `Auth::guard(...)->logout()`, which both logout paths call *before* `$request->session()->invalidate()`, and `invalidate()` flushes all session attributes, silently destroying whatever the listener had just written. Moved to firing after invalidate instead: a new `App\Http\Responses\LogoutResponse` (customer, bound via Fortify's `LogoutResponse` contract, builds its response after `AuthenticatedSessionController::destroy()`'s own invalidate call) and a direct call in `AdminAuthController::logout()` (after its own invalidate/regenerateToken calls). **Not verified in a live browser this session** — no browser automation tool was available; the test suite proves the session flag and middleware wiring are correct, but an actual back-button check would add confidence. This gap is exactly how the follow-up bug below reached the user instead of being caught here.

**Follow-up (`7addfdf`):** owner reported the admin flash-sales page stuck "loading" and a console error, `Uncaught (in promise) Error: Unable to encrypt history`. Root cause: history encryption needs `window.crypto.subtle`, a secure-context-only API (HTTPS, or the `localhost`/`127.0.0.1` exception) — the owner's dev server is reached over plain HTTP via a LAN IP (`vite.config.ts`'s `192.168.0.111:8001`), which is neither. Confirmed by reading `node_modules/@inertiajs/core/dist/index.js`: `encryptHistory()` calls `createKey()`, which returns `null` (not a throw, just a `console.warn`) when `crypto.subtle` is undefined — but `encryptHistory()` itself then explicitly throws `"Unable to encrypt history"` on that null key, as an uncaught promise rejection inside the visit-completion chain, which is why the *page* got stuck, not just the console. Fix: new `ConditionallyEncryptHistory` middleware only calls `Inertia::encryptHistory()` when `$request->secure()`. `Inertia::clearHistory()` (the logout half) was confirmed separately *not* to touch any crypto API client-side, so it needed no equivalent gating.

Two implementation gotchas hit while fixing this, both worth keeping in mind:
- Registering the fix under the *same* alias name inertia-laravel uses (`inertia.encrypt`) doesn't override it — tried first, verified broken via `app('router')->getMiddleware()['inertia.encrypt']` still resolving to the package's own class. The package's service provider registers that alias from its own `boot()`, which runs after `bootstrap/app.php`'s middleware configuration and wins the conflict. Used a different alias name (`history.encrypt`) instead, referenced directly in `routes/account.php`/`routes/admin.php`.
- Testing "a secure request" by passing `withServerVariables(['HTTPS' => 'on'])` to a plain `route()`-generated `http://` URL doesn't work — confirmed via `Log::debug()` inside the middleware that `$request->secure()` still read `false`. Symfony's `Request::create()` derives `HTTPS` from the URL's own scheme and explicitly `unset()`s whatever was passed in `$server` if the URL isn't `https://` (`vendor/symfony/http-foundation/Request.php`, the `create()` method). The URL itself has to be `https://` (`str_replace('http://', 'https://', route(...))`) for the simulated request to actually read as secure.

## BIZ-030 notes

Pure frontend label fix, no backend change — `order.payment_status` was already shared to this page. `OrderConfirmation.tsx` now shows "Total Paid" only when actually paid, "Total Refunded" when refunded, and "Total Due on Delivery" for an unpaid COD order (previously always "Total Paid" regardless, risking courier-door disputes when a COD customer believed they'd already paid). Copy/display-only change — no test added, per CLAUDE.md's exemption for pure copy/styling changes.

## SEC-003 notes

Root cause confirmed by reading `EmailVerificationCodeController::store()`: the submitted code is checked only against `$user->email_verification_code` (a hash, bound to the user ROW, not to any specific email address). `Settings\ProfileController::update()` nulled `email_verified_at` on an email change but left the pending code fields untouched, so a code issued for the OLD address stayed valid and could be replayed against the user's row after it now points at a different (unproven) address. Fix is the minimal, root-cause one: clear all three code fields (`email_verification_code`, `_expires_at`, `_sent_at`) in the same `isDirty('email')` branch that already clears `email_verified_at`. Verified this actually closes the exploit, not just the DB-state symptom: the test captures a genuine code via a faked mail, changes the email, then submits that exact code and confirms it's rejected (`assertSessionHasErrors('code')`), not merely that the column reads null.

## SEC-002 notes

`SettingController::settingsProp()` called `Setting::all()` for every one of the six settings pages — the per-page `admin.can:<section>,view` middleware controls who can *open* a page, but every page that successfully opened received the entire settings table regardless, including Pathao/Steadfast credentials and Google/Facebook OAuth secrets. The authorization half of this was already correct and already had the right shape to reuse: `FIELD_GROUPS` (used by `authorizeFieldGroups()` to gate `update()` per field group) already lists exactly the fields each of 5 pages needs. Added one more constant, `GENERAL_FIELDS`, for the 6th page (`edit()`, the one not in `FIELD_GROUPS` because its fields fall back to the generic `settings` permission) — derived by reading exactly which `settings.*` keys `edit.tsx` references, the same method used for each other page. `settingsProp()` now takes that list and queries `Setting::whereIn('key', $keys)` instead of `all()`. No page's own fields were affected — confirmed by the full existing settings-pages test suite (108 tests) passing unchanged, plus the new test proving the leak itself is closed (a limited admin's about-page response no longer contains the courier/OAuth secrets).

## SEC-006 notes

Same bug FSA-03 already fixed once (the order-confirmation page sent the raw model) had two more instances: `OrderTrackingController::show()` (reachable by anyone who has an order number plus the phone or email on the courier's own parcel label — no login) and `LandingPageController::confirmation()` (reachable by whoever holds the unguessable `guest_token`, lower severity but the same raw-model habit). Both built an explicit allow-list from reading exactly what `TrackOrder.tsx`/`LandingConfirmation.tsx` use (same method as SEC-002's per-page field lists). The most concrete harm closed: `OrderStatusHistory.notes` sometimes embeds the acting admin's real name (`RefundService::record()` writes `"Marked as refunded by {$admin->name}: …"`), which `OrderTimeline.tsx` renders verbatim — anyone with an order number and the phone off the parcel label could see a staff member's name with zero authentication. Deliberately left alone: `account/OrderDetail.tsx`, the *logged-in* customer viewing their own order, uses the same `OrderTimeline` component against the same unfiltered notes — same underlying issue, but a different, narrower one (the question there is whether a customer should see internal staff names on their own order, not whether a stranger can), and it wasn't in this finding's scope.

## SEC-004 notes

`SocialAccount::token`/`refresh_token` had no `$hidden` and nothing in the app ever reads them back (Socialite's tokens are used once, at the OAuth callback, then stored but never retrieved — confirmed by grepping for `->token`/`->refresh_token` reads anywhere other than the write site in `SocialAuthController`). The subtle half of this finding: `Settings\ConnectionsController::edit()` does `$user->socialAccounts->map(...)` — and `$user` there is `$request->user()`, the *exact same* object `HandleInertiaRequests::share()` later assigns to the globally-shared `auth.user` prop. Eloquent caches a lazy-loaded relation on the instance it was loaded onto, so visiting `/settings/connections` would have left the shared `auth.user` prop (rendered on literally every subsequent page in that response, not just this one) carrying the full, unhidden `SocialAccount` rows — even though the page's *own* `connections` prop already mapped to a safe `{id, provider, connected_at}` shape. `$hidden` fixes both at once since it's enforced at serialization, not at the call site.

## SEC-005 notes

Confirmed by reading Laravel's actual `AuthenticateSession::handle()`: it calls `$request->user()` with no guard argument and compares against `session('password_hash_'.$this->auth->getDefaultDriver())` — both hardcoded to the app's *default* guard (`web` here), with no supported way to parameterize it for a second guard via the plain `auth.session` alias. First design attempt wired the admin side's rotation through an `Illuminate\Auth\Events\Logout` listener in `AppServiceProvider` — wrong, because that event fires *during* `Auth::guard(...)->logout()`, which both `AdminAuthController::logout()` and Fortify's `AuthenticatedSessionController::destroy()` call *before* `session()->invalidate()`, and `invalidate()` flushes all session attributes — so anything the listener had just written was destroyed moments later, before any subsequent request could read it. Moved the write to *after* invalidate instead: a new `App\Http\Responses\LogoutResponse` (bound via Fortify's `LogoutResponse` contract, same pattern as the existing `LoginResponse`/`RegisterResponse`) for customers, and a direct call in `AdminAuthController::logout()` for admin.

Second design issue, found by running the full `tests/Feature/Admin/` suite after the first cut (comparing hash alone): 9 tests failed, all sharing one pattern — `actingAs($adminA, 'admin')` then `actingAs($adminB, 'admin')` within the same test method, which shares one session without ever calling a real logout. `Auth::login()` itself doesn't guard against this either (logging in as a different account on a browser already authenticated as someone else, without an explicit logout first, is a real if unusual path). Fixed by keying the stored fingerprint on **admin id + password hash**, not the hash alone: a session that starts tracking a *different* admin than it last saw adopts the new identity silently (no logout), and only "same admin id, different hash" — the actual SEC-005 scenario, this exact account's password changed since this session last confirmed it — triggers the forced logout. This resolved all 9 failures without touching any of those test files.

## SEC-010 notes

All four sub-fixes read current behavior before changing it, to avoid assuming: (1) `Password::min(8)` → `Password::default()` — read `Password::default()`'s own source (`vendor/laravel/framework`) and confirmed it falls back to `Password::min(8)` whenever the registered factory callback returns `null`, which `AppServiceProvider::configureDefaults()`'s factory does outside production — so this fix is behaviorally invisible in every environment except production, where the factory instead returns the full strengthened rule. (2) `current_password:admin` only gates a **self**-edit with a password field filled; `AdminController::update()` already distinguishes self-edit (`$currentAdmin->id === $admin->id`) for the unrelated permissions-mutation guard, reused the same flag. (3) Read `FortifyServiceProvider`'s customer-facing `'login'` limiter for comparison — it has the *identical* gap (email+IP and IP-only, no email-only), so this is a pre-existing, sitewide limitation, not something specific to the admin guard; the finding scoped itself to admin specifically (higher stakes — "admin takeover allows full store control"), so only `admin-login` was changed here, not the customer limiter. (4) Logging only fires after `attempt()` has already failed, and logs the email + IP, never the password.

## FE-005 notes

Read `AppServiceProvider::configureErrorPages()` first: `if ($status >= 500) { Inertia::flushShared(); }`, with the comment explaining why — "the database or shared props may be exactly what failed." Confirmed server-side with a test (`ErrorPagesTest.php`, `.missing('auth')` added to the existing 500-page assertion) that `auth` is genuinely absent from a 500 response's props, not present-as-null. `resources/js/app.tsx`'s `router.on('navigate', ...)` handler read `props.auth?.user?.id ?? null` — optional chaining happily reduces "the key doesn't exist" and "the key is `{user: null}`" to the exact same `null`, so a 500 on *any* page during a logged-in session looked indistinguishable from that customer logging out, and `applyLogout()` cleared their cart (and the empty cart then synced to the server, deleting the real rows too — confirmed by reading `CartManager::replaceItems()`'s tail, though no code there needed to change; it correctly did what an empty `items` payload told it to do, the payload itself was the bug). Fixed by checking `'auth' in props` before the whole block, not just the logout branch — an error page should leave cart/wishlist/auth-tracking state exactly as it was, not partially update some of it. **Not verified in a live browser this session** — no browser automation tool was available.

## FSA-02 notes (= SEC-001, 2026-11-05 audit)

**Status: CLOSED, 2026-11-05. Code deployed; sender rules configured in production by the owner. Owner closed this at their own instruction, without a separate post-change real-payment confirmation — if a real bKash/Nagad payment is later seen to fail to verify, that's this same finding reopened, not a new one.**

- **Root cause:** `SmsParserService::senderAllowed()` treats a `null`/empty `sender_regex` as "allow any sender" — the allowlist mechanism existed in code but was opt-in, and no `sms_patterns` row had ever had one set, so in practice *any* SMS whose body matched the identifier/amount/TrxID pattern was accepted regardless of who sent it. Anyone texting the shop's forwarder number a fabricated "payment received" message, from their own ordinary phone, could mark an order as paid.
- **Why this took two attempts to get the value right:** first asked the owner for the sender, and received a transaction ID (`TrxID`/`TxnID` values) instead — a different field entirely, extracted from inside the SMS body. Clarified the distinction (sender = who the *text message itself* is from; shown at the top of the phone's own messaging app / SMS thread, never written inside the message content) and asked again. Second attempt returned the Nagad message's own `Sender: 018xxxxxxxx` body field — which is the *paying customer's* phone number (Nagad's own courtesy inclusion in the notification text), not the SMS sender — using that value would have made the rule match only that one customer and reject every other genuine payment. Clarified again, explicitly asking what the phone's own Messages app shows as "from" for that conversation: `bKash` and `NAGAD`, exactly the short alphanumeric IDs these providers are expected to send from.
- **Fix applied (data only, no code change — the enforcement mechanism was already deployed):** owner set, via Admin › SMS Patterns:
  - The `bkash` pattern (identifier `TrxID`): `sender_regex` = `(bkash)`
  - Both `nagad` patterns (identifiers `Money Received.` and `Cash In Received.`): `sender_regex` = `(nagad)`
  - Deliberately a substring match, not an exact `^...$` match: tolerant of the forwarder app relaying the sender field slightly differently than the phone's own display, while a normal customer phone number (digits only) can never contain those letters either way. Case-insensitive by `SmsRegex::run()`'s own `/i` flag, applied to every pattern regardless of casing typed.
  - The admin form's `SafeSmsRegex` rule requires a capture group in parentheses even for a sender field that's only ever used for a boolean match (`SmsRegex::matches()`, never `firstCapture()`) — without it, `(` `)`, the form rejects the value as invalid. Confirmed via `SmsRegex::problem()`.
- **Verified before handing the values to the owner (local, read-only):** `SmsRegex::problem('(bkash)')` / `problem('(nagad)')` both `null` (valid); `SmsRegex::matches('(bkash)', 'bKash')` and `matches('(nagad)', 'NAGAD')` both `true`; `matches('(bkash)', '01797117509')` and `matches('(nagad)', '01837418940')` (the real customer phone numbers from the owner's own example messages) both `false` — confirms a forged SMS from an ordinary phone is still rejected under the new rule.
- **Closed by owner instruction, 2026-11-05**, before a real payment was confirmed to still verify correctly since the rule was saved — a deliberate choice, not an oversight. If a real bKash/Nagad payment is later seen to fail to verify, treat it as this same finding reopened.

## FSA-01 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(security): harden product variants, pricing and visibility (FSA-01, FSA-04, FSA-05)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `PlaceOrderRequest` and `AddToCartRequest` validated `variant_id` only with `exists:product_variants,id`. `SyncCartRequest` and `Admin/CreateOrderRequest` already checked ownership. `CartManager::resolvePrice()` priced from `product_id` (`FlashSaleItem::findActiveFor()` matches a product-wide sale against any variant id), while `InventoryService` locked and decremented the variant by id alone, and the order line took that variant's SKU.
- **Fix, all layers fail closed:**
  - New `app/Rules/VariantBelongsToProduct.php` (data-aware; derives the sibling `product_id` key, so it works for `variant_id` and `items.N.variant_id`). Applied in `PlaceOrderRequest` and `AddToCartRequest`. Same message as `SyncCartRequest`'s existing closure, which was deliberately left unchanged.
  - `CartManager::resolvePrice()` loads the variant scoped to the product before any flash-sale lookup: a mismatch is `ModelNotFoundException` (404), the same outcome as the existing missing-variant path.
  - `CartManager::validItems()` drops a cart row whose variant belongs to another product, as it already did for deleted products, so a row stored before the fix is never priced or checked out.
  - `InventoryService::decrementForOrderItem()` throws `InsufficientStockException::forMismatchedVariant()` before touching stock. Every order path (COD, mobile banking, landing page, admin) already catches this exception and rolls back. Exception: `Admin\IncompleteOrderController::placeOrder()` catches no stock exception at all (pre-existing, unchanged), so there a mismatch rolls back as a 500.
- **Files:** `app/Rules/VariantBelongsToProduct.php` (new), `app/Http/Requests/PlaceOrderRequest.php`, `app/Http/Requests/AddToCartRequest.php`, `app/Support/CartManager.php`, `app/Services/InventoryService.php`, `app/Exceptions/InsufficientStockException.php`, `tests/Feature/VariantProductOwnershipTest.php` (new).
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):**
  - `VariantProductOwnershipTest` 8/8 (24 assertions): foreign variant via `cart.store`; authenticated and guest `checkout.store` (no order, stock unchanged); valid pairs still work through both endpoints (variant price charged, stock decremented); a pre-existing mismatched cart row is not checked out; service guards in `resolvePrice()` and `InventoryService`.
  - The audit's proof-of-concept probe (a ৳50,000 variant ordered for ৳100) now fails to create an order.
  - Regression sweep of 70 cart, checkout, flash-sale, bundle, inventory, stock, landing-page, mobile-payment, wishlist, coupon, loyalty, referral and order test files: 709 tests, 679 passed, 30 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Pint passed.
  - MySQL race files grepped: only `CartConcurrencyRaceTest.php:170` passes a variant, and it pairs it with its own product.
- **Full SQLite suite (2026-11-03, together with FSA-03/04):** 1687 tests, 1624 passed, 63 skipped, 0 failed. **Not run:** MySQL tests (no MySQL-specific behaviour changed).
- **Follow-up found during FSA-05:** a mixed cart containing a pre-fix mismatched row was refused by `lockForCheckout()` instead of dropping that row. This was fixed under FSA-05 (see FSA-05 notes), so for a clean FSA-01 commit `lockForCheckout()` belongs with FSA-05.
- **Remaining:** commit and deploy, each with separate approval. After deploy, the owner runs the read-only mismatch query from report §6 against production to find any orders placed through this hole earlier.

## FSA-03 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(checkout): secure order confirmation and preserve bundle attribution (FSA-03, FSA-12)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `routes/commerce.php`'s `order.confirmation` closure had no middleware and no ownership check, and rendered `$order->load('items.product')`. `Order` had no `$hidden`, so the full shipping address including phone, totals, coupon and points data, courier ids and `guest_token` went to anyone who knew the order number. Order numbers are printed in emails, SMS and courier labels.
- **Fix:**
  - New `app/Support/OrderConfirmationAccess.php`:
    - `allows()`: true for the logged-in owner (`orders.user_id`), or for a session holding an unexpired grant for that order number.
    - `grant()`: stores `order_number => expiry` in the session, 24 hours, at most 20 entries, expired entries pruned.
    - `payload()`: an explicit allow-list of exactly the fields `OrderConfirmation.tsx` renders. These are order number, status, payment method, total, `created_at`, and shipping name, email and address lines. Excluded: phone, items, user id, tokens, courier ids.
  - Route: `abort_unless(allows, 404)`; it uses a 404 so a guessed number reveals nothing. It renders `payload()` and adds `throttle:order-tracking`, the existing admin-tunable "order lookup" limiter, default 30/min.
  - Grants are recorded at every redirect that follows a successful placement or payment:
    - `CheckoutController`: COD success and the cached `order_token` replay. The token is a per-render secret, so a replay comes from the original buyer.
    - `MobilePaymentController`: `show()` and `submit()` when the pending checkout is already converted, and after a successful submit.
    - `PaymentController` (legacy ZiniPay) is auth-only and owner-checked, so the owner rule covers it unchanged.
  - `Order::$hidden = ['guest_token']`, defence in depth for every serialized order (account order pages, etc.). `LandingPageController::confirmation()` already passes `guestToken` explicitly to its holder, so the landing-page flow is unchanged.
- **Files:** `app/Support/OrderConfirmationAccess.php` (new), `routes/commerce.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/CheckoutController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/MobilePaymentController.php`, `app/Models/Order.php`, `tests/Feature/OrderConfirmationAccessTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `OrderConfirmationAccessTest` 9/9 (50 assertions) on SQLite:
  - The owner sees only the allow-listed fields: no phone, items, user id or `guest_token`, checked in the page HTML too.
  - The owner from a new session is allowed.
  - A guest in the placing session is allowed.
  - An anonymous visitor gets 404, and another logged-in customer gets 404.
  - A guest grant expires after 24 hours.
  - A cached-token replay re-grants access without a second order.
  - A guest who pays by mobile banking is allowed, and a fresh session gets 404.
  - `account.orders.show` never contains `guest_token`.
- **Gaps and limits:**
  - A guest who returns to the confirmation page after 24 hours, or from another device, gets 404 and must use order tracking (phone or email). This is intended.
  - The invoice link on the confirmation page still points at `account.orders.invoice` (auth-only, owner-checked), unchanged.
  - The frontend `Order` TypeScript type is broader than the payload; the page reads only allow-listed fields.

## FSA-04 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(security): harden product variants, pricing and visibility (FSA-01, FSA-04, FSA-05)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `Product` and `ProductVariant` had no `$hidden`. Every storefront surface serializes the models whole: product detail (`toArray()`, variants, related products), listings and search, the shared `cart` prop on every page, Buy Now on checkout, and the order confirmation. So `cost_price` reached every visitor.
- **Fix:** `protected $hidden = ['cost_price']` on both models. This is one rule for every current and future serialization; per-controller column lists would miss new props. `Admin\ProductController::edit()`, the only screen that renders cost (`edit.tsx`, `ProductVariantManager.tsx`), calls `makeVisible('cost_price')` on the product and each variant. Admin create posts cost without reading a model. Saving is unaffected, because `$hidden` only affects serialization; mass assignment, the audit log and `ProductStockToken` read attributes directly.
- **Files:** `app/Models/Product.php`, `app/Models/ProductVariant.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/ProductController.php`, `tests/Feature/CostPriceVisibilityTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `CostPriceVisibilityTest` 5/5 (50 assertions):
  - Product detail has no product or variant `cost_price`, in props or anywhere in the page.
  - Listing and search pages show the product but contain no `cost_price`.
  - Cart page and checkout Buy Now, including the shared cart prop, contain no `cost_price`.
  - The admin edit page still receives the product and variant `cost_price`.
  - The audit's earlier probe proved the leak existed on product detail.
- **Full SQLite suite after FSA-01/03/04:** 1687 tests, 1624 passed, 63 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Pint passed; it touched only the new lines. MySQL race files reference none of the changed routes or attributes.
- **Remaining:** cached pre-fix arrays (layout, search-suggestion and catalog-filter caches) are cleared by deploy's `cache:clear`. Admin activity-log views still show `cost_price` changes, which is admin-only and intended.

## FSA-05 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(security): harden product variants, pricing and visibility (FSA-01, FSA-04, FSA-05)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** listings, search, home, brands and landing pages already filter `active()->published()`, but three entry points did not:
  - `ProductController::show()` bound the product by slug with no visibility check.
  - `CartManager::validItems()` dropped only deleted products.
  - Checkout's Buy Now preview (`resolveBuyNowItem()`) and `AddToCartRequest` accepted any existing product id.
- **Fix:**
  - New `Product::isPubliclyVisible()`, the instance form of `active()->published()`: active, `published_at` set and not in the future.
  - `ProductController::show()` returns 404 for a hidden product.
  - `CartManager::validItems()` also drops hidden products. A stale cart row or a submitted hidden item is never priced or sold, exactly like a deleted product today: an all-hidden cart gets the existing 422 "Your cart is empty"; a mixed cart orders only the visible lines (the established `CartStaleProductTest` behaviour).
  - `PlaceOrderRequest` validation deliberately left unchanged: `Checkout.tsx` has no display for per-item errors, so a stale client cart would be stuck with an invisible error, whereas the drop path is already handled.
  - `AddToCartRequest` rejects a hidden or deleted product with "This product is not available." (`Rule::exists` with the same conditions).
  - Buy Now ignores a hidden product, so checkout redirects home.
- **Defect found and fixed during FSA-05, which also affected FSA-01:** `CartManager::lockForCheckout()` (MED-08) re-checked the locked cart with only `whereHas('product')`, not the full `validItems()` filter. A mixed cart containing any row checkout deliberately dropped (a foreign variant from FSA-01, or a hidden product) therefore failed with "Your cart changed…" every time, and the row was never removed, so the customer was stuck. It still failed closed. `lockForCheckout()` now applies `validItems()` to the locked rows, so the two can't diverge. `CartConcurrencyTest` and `CartStaleProductTest` pass.
- **Admin preview:** none exists. Logged-in admins are redirected off the storefront by `RedirectAdminToDashboard`, so there was nothing to preserve.
- **Not changed (out of scope, noted):**
  - Admin manual orders and incomplete-order conversion can still order hidden products; that is an admin decision.
  - A mobile-banking `PendingCheckout` staged before a product was hidden still converts at payment, because the customer has already paid.
  - The wishlist page still lists hidden products, but they can't be added to the cart or bought.
- **Test-support changes:**
  - `database/factories/ProductFactory.php` now defaults `published_at` to yesterday. Admin create and update always set `published_at = now()` for an active product, so the old "active but never published" default was a state real data can't have, and it is hidden under the storefront's own rule. Tests wanting a draft pass `published_at => null`.
  - `tests/Feature/ReferralProgramTest.php:468,490`: two time-travel tests that move the clock back to 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-01 now pin the fixture's `published_at` to 2026-01-01. Otherwise the product is correctly "not yet published" at the travelled-to time.
- **Files:** `app/Models/Product.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/ProductController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/CheckoutController.php`, `app/Support/CartManager.php`, `app/Http/Requests/AddToCartRequest.php`, `database/factories/ProductFactory.php`, `tests/Feature/ProductVisibilityTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/ReferralProgramTest.php`.
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):** `ProductVisibilityTest` 18/18:
  - A visible page loads.
  - Product page 404, add-to-cart rejection, authenticated checkout with no order and stock untouched, guest mixed checkout charging only the visible line, and Buy Now redirect: each for the inactive, draft and future-scheduled states.
  - A future product becomes visible after its time.
  - A cart row hidden after adding can't be checked out.
- **Full suite:** 1705 tests, 1642 passed, 63 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Pint passed. MySQL race files contain no visibility-dependent fixtures or `lockForCheckout` call-site assumptions. MySQL tests were not run.

## FSA-06 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Option A approved by owner (refuse cancel of paid orders, direct to the refund flow). Committed locally (`fe6fd58`); not pushed, not deployed.**

- **Root cause:** `Admin\OrderController::cancel()` (`:406-451`) checked `status` (`UNCANCELLABLE_STATUSES` plus the FSA-11 `SHIPPED` guard) but never `payment_status`. A mobile-banking order (created `paid` at checkout, per `OrderService.php:58`) could be cancelled while still paid; `RefundService::assertRefundable()` then refuses to refund an already-`cancelled` order, stranding the money with no ledger path. `DashboardController::collectedRevenueQuery()`'s cancelled-order exclusion also silently dropped it from revenue reporting (the `LOW-08` cross-reference already flagged this interaction).
- **Fix:** a `$lockedOrder->payment_status === PaymentStatus::PAID` check inside `cancel()`'s existing locked transaction, placed after the FSA-11 `SHIPPED` check (so a shipped+paid order still gets the shipped message) and before the courier-unresolved check. New `OrderController::PAID_CANCEL_MESSAGE` constant. `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx`'s Cancel Order card condition gained `order.payment_status !== 'paid'`. `RefundService` untouched — no refund logic duplicated.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderController.php`, `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx`, `tests/Feature/ShippedOrderCancellationTest.php`.
- **Tests:** `ShippedOrderCancellationTest` 10/10 (7 existing + 3 new parametrized cases: paid pending/processing orders refused with nothing reversed; pre-existing shipped-order and unpaid-COD-can-still-cancel coverage unchanged).
- **Known gap, not implemented:** no frontend test framework exists in this repo (no vitest/jest, no `.test.tsx` files, no `test` script in `package.json`) — the JSX condition change was verified by reading, not by an automated render assertion.
- **Conflict found while implementing FSA-07 (2026-11-03), resolved the same day (owner-approved Option 1):** `tests/Feature/MobilePaymentMatchingTest.php:310` ("a transaction consumed by an order that is later cancelled still cannot be reused for a new order") originally cancelled a paid mobile-banking order via `admin.orders.cancel`, which the FSA-06 fix now correctly refuses. The test's actual subject — a transaction id already consumed by one order can never be reused for another, regardless of that order's later status — is independent of *how* the order became cancelled, so the fix is test-setup-only: the cancelled state is now established directly against the model (`$order->update(['status' => OrderStatus::CANCELLED])`), with an inline comment explaining why (FSA-06 now correctly blocks the admin-endpoint path this test used to use). The duplicate-transaction assertion itself (`assertSessionHasErrors('transaction_id')`, order count stays 1, `$sms->matched_order_id` unchanged) is untouched. No FSA-06 production code was changed to accommodate this. Now 17/17.
- **Second, identical conflict found by the 2026-11-03 final pre-release audit (fresh full-suite run), fixed as a dedicated regression-cleanup task, same day:** this was the third and fourth times the same root cause surfaced — a pre-existing test that cancels a *paid* order via `admin.orders.cancel` and expects success, which FSA-06 now correctly refuses. Confirmed by direct code reading that the production rule (`PaymentStatus::PAID` → refuse, unchanged) is correct; only the stale tests needed fixing, per owner-approved scope.
  - `tests/Feature/OrderCancellationAtomicityTest.php`'s "a cancelled order cannot then be refunded, and its redeemed points are credited only once" cancelled a paid order via `admin.orders.cancel` expecting `assertSessionHas('success')`. Fixed by cancelling via the courier-report entry point instead (`CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync($order, 'steadfast', 'cancelled_parcells')`) — one of this same test file's other two documented cancellation entry points — which FSA-06 doesn't touch, preserving the test's actual point (a cancelled order cannot then be refunded, points credited exactly once) regardless of which entry point cancelled it. Confirmed fixed: now 10/10 on SQLite.
  - `tests/Feature/LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest.php` (MySQL-only, skips on SQLite) had the identical conflict baked into its shared `createRedeemedPointsRaceOrder()` fixture (hardcoded `payment_status: 'paid'`) in three of its eight call sites — the three that race `cancel()` itself against another action (duplicate cancel, cancel vs. mark-refunded, cancel vs. a courier report). The other five call sites (duplicate mark-refunded, ledger-rollback, deadlock-retry, and two courier-webhook races) never call `cancel()` and genuinely need the paid fixture for `markRefunded()` to apply at all, so the fixture itself was left at its default and the function was given an optional `$overrides` parameter instead; only the three affected tests now pass `['payment_status' => 'unpaid', 'payment_method' => 'cod']`.
  - No production application code was changed for either fix — both are test-only corrections to match the already-approved FSA-06 business rule.
- **MySQL execution of the `LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest.php` fixture correction (2026-11-03, owner-provided local credentials — the "the three tests are still unexecuted" statement from the final pre-release audit and the first regression-cleanup task is now superseded by this):**
  - **Environment:** local MySQL 8.0.46 server (`127.0.0.1:3306`), confirmed via `SELECT VERSION()`. This is a local development MySQL instance, distinct from production (which is a remote MariaDB 11.8.9 instance this workspace has no credentials for at all — see FSA-06's own production-verification caveat below; this local run proves nothing about production).
  - **Isolated test database:** a dedicated `shoppydrive_test_fsa` database was created (`utf8mb4`/`utf8mb4_unicode_ci`), matching `Tests\SafeTestDatabase`'s required `shoppydrive_test_` prefix — confirmed directly: `SafeTestDatabase::assertSafe('mysql', config('database.connections.mysql'))` returned without throwing for this database name, and the guard remained enabled throughout (it is unconditionally invoked from `TestCase::setUpTraits()` before every test). A scoped user (`shoppydrive_test_fsa_user`@`127.0.0.1`) limited to `shoppydrive_test_fsa.*` only was also created and confirmed, by direct test, unable to access the real `ShoppyDrive` development database (`Access denied ... to database 'ShoppyDrive'`).
  - **One privilege gap, handled transparently:** the race tests' own lock-monitoring query (`SELECT ... FROM information_schema.INNODB_TRX WHERE trx_state = 'LOCK WAIT'`) requires the global `PROCESS` privilege, which MySQL cannot scope to a single database — the dedicated scoped user therefore couldn't run them. Granting that privilege was treated as a sensitive action requiring explicit owner approval and was not pushed through. Instead, the test execution used the explicitly owner-authorized local MySQL `root` credentials directly, with `DB_DATABASE` (and the test file's own `LOYALTY_RACE_TEST_DATABASE` env var) both forced to `shoppydrive_test_fsa` — so every migration, write and query in the run was still confined to that one isolated database, never `ShoppyDrive` or any other.
  - **Confirmed untouched:** the real `ShoppyDrive` development database was checked before and after this run — 73 tables, unchanged.
  - **Results:** the 3 adjusted cases run individually — **3/3 passed, 21 assertions**. The complete `LoyaltyPointsConcurrencyRaceTest.php` file run afterward (per the file's own convention of running the full suite once the targeted cases are confirmed) — **12/12 passed, 70 assertions**. Each `php artisan test` invocation paid a one-time `migrate:fresh` cost against the freshly-created schema (not cached between separate process invocations), which is why these runs took several minutes of wall-clock time; this is a local-environment performance characteristic, not a test or application concern.
  - **Not claimed:** production verification. This is local MySQL 8.0.46 evidence only — production is a separate, remote MariaDB 11.8.9 instance this workspace cannot reach, and no claim is made that these results transfer directly to it (the same caveat the 2026-11-03 full-system audit already recorded for the 8 already-deployed findings' own MySQL verification).

## FSA-07 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Two of three owner-approved increments built; the third (durable PendingCheckout↔SMS linking, MED-20 Tier 2 style) was explicitly not built per owner instruction.**

- **Root cause:** `MobilePaymentController::submit()` checks stock, coupon cap and `PendingCheckout` expiry only at submit time, after the customer has already paid. Every failure path throws inside the `DB::transaction()`, rolling back the order — but the `received_sms` row (committed independently, earlier) survives unmatched, and the `PendingCheckout` row survives retry-able. Confirmed via existing coverage (`tests/Feature/InventoryEnforcementTest.php:288`) that retry already works mechanically; the real gap is recoverability/visibility, not data loss.
- **Increment 1 — expiry grace for proven payment (implemented):** in `MobilePaymentController::submit()`, both the outer and the locked inner expiry check now also pass when a `ReceivedSms` matching the submitted `(provider, transaction_id)` has `received_at` between the `PendingCheckout`'s own `created_at` and its original `expires_at` (new private `hasProofOfPaymentWithinWindow()`). `expires_at` itself is never written anywhere by this change — not extended globally or per-row. An SMS received after the original `expires_at` is still refused. All existing matching rules (amount, duplicate-use, wrong-provider) run exactly as before once past this check.
- **Increment 2 — admin unmatched-SMS badge (implemented):** `Admin\ReceivedSmsController::poll()` (the existing 5-second toast-polling endpoint, `useSmsPolling.ts`) now also returns `unmatched_count` — unmatched SMS older than `UNMATCHED_STALE_MINUTES = 30` (comfortably shorter than the 2-hour checkout window; chosen because normal customers complete the TrxID step in minutes). Surfaced as the existing sidebar-badge pattern (same component already used for `unseenOrdersCount`) on the "Received SMS" nav item. No new route, table, or dashboard.
- **Increment 3 — durable linking (deliberately not built):** matches this tracker's own `MED-20` Tier 2 precedent (a `PendingCheckout` with no `order_number` is ambiguous between "abandoned" and "paid but failed," and resolving that needs a schema decision this tracker doesn't make unilaterally). Not implemented per explicit owner instruction.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/MobilePaymentController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/ReceivedSmsController.php`, `resources/js/hooks/useSmsPolling.ts`, `resources/js/layouts/admin-layout.tsx`, `tests/Feature/MobilePaymentMatchingTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Admin/SmsPollReconciliationTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `MobilePaymentMatchingTest` 17/17 (all green, including the FSA-06 conflict resolved per that section's notes above) — new: late-submission-within-window accepted and `expires_at` unchanged; SMS received after original expiry still refused; genuinely-expired-with-no-SMS still refused. `SmsPollReconciliationTest` 3/3 (new): stale unmatched counted, matched excluded, recent-unmatched excluded, existing SMS list unchanged.
- **Not claimed:** FSA-02 (SMS sender authenticity) remains unresolved — this work does not touch or depend on sender trust, and does not make FSA-02 better or worse.

## FSA-08 notes

**Status: IMPLEMENTED_LOCALLY, COMMITTED, NOT PUSHED, 2026-11-03. Block-at-admin-save approved by owner.**

- **Root cause:** `Admin\LandingPageController::store()`/`update()` validated `product_id` only with `exists:products,id`. `LandingPageController::completeLandingPageOrder()` always builds `variant_id => null`, and `InventoryService::decrementForOrderItem()` (`:76-78`) throws deterministically for any variant product ordered with no `variant_id` — every order through a landing page for such a product fails 100% of the time (COD fails immediately with no money collected; mobile banking is FSA-07's deterministic case, money already collected first).
- **Fix:** a closure validation rule (`LandingPageController::productHasVariantsRule()`) added to both `store()` and `update()`, rejecting a `product_id` where `Product::whereKey($value)->whereHas('variants')->exists()`. No variant selector or funnel redesign built, per instruction.
- **Pre-implementation read-only check (as instructed), on the local dev MySQL database — not production:** `SELECT lp.id, lp.title, lp.slug, lp.product_id, lp.is_active, p.name, variant_count ...` found **one existing affected row**: landing page id `4`, slug `test`, `product_id` `64` ("Chomolokko Products", 4 variants), **`is_active = 1`**. **Not modified, deactivated, or deleted** — read-only per instruction.
- **Consequence for that existing page, not silently assumed away:** `update()` re-validates `product_id` on every save regardless of whether it changed, so this specific page can no longer be saved via the normal edit form (including an unrelated change like fixing a typo) until its product is changed to a variant-less one — confirmed by a dedicated test (`LandingPageVariantRejectionTest`: "saving other fields on a landing page already pointed at a variant product is blocked until the product changes"). It can still be deactivated or deleted without hitting this validation, since `updateStatusMany()`/`destroyMany()` don't validate `product_id` at all — confirmed by reading `UpdatesStatusInBulk`. **Owner decision needed:** leave page `4` as-is (broken, active, un-editable until repointed), deactivate it via the existing bulk-action UI, or repoint/delete it. No action taken pending that decision. **This is a local development-database record only — whether the same or a similar row exists on production is unknown and not claimed either way; that would need a separate, explicit production check.**
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/LandingPageController.php`, `tests/Feature/Admin/LandingPageVariantRejectionTest.php` (new).
- **Tests:** `LandingPageVariantRejectionTest` 4/4 (new: create rejected for a variant product, create still works for a variant-less one, repointing an existing page at a variant product rejected, saving unrelated fields on an already-variant-pointed page blocked); `LandingPageCustomContentValidationTest` 5/5 (regression, unaffected — its fixture product has no variants).

## FSA-09 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(auth): prevent social login account takeover (FSA-09)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `SocialAuthController::callback()` linked a social identity to any existing `User` with the same email. The only refusal (SEC-01) was Google's explicit `email_verified === false`. A missing claim, and every Facebook login, linked. The local account's own `email_verified_at` was never consulted, and the password and sessions of whoever created that account survived. Two related paths:
  - New Facebook accounts were created `email_verified_at = now()` on Facebook's word.
  - `connectToCurrentUser()` let an unverified account attach providers (`settings/connections` requires only `auth`). A pre-registrant could plant a Facebook link that outlived the real owner verifying the address.
- **Provider guarantees, as integrated:**
  - Google (Socialite `GoogleProvider`, OpenID userinfo) returns a boolean `email_verified` claim in the raw payload.
  - Socialite's `FacebookProvider` requests `name, email, gender, verified, link, picture`. `verified` is a deprecated account-level flag, not an email-verification claim, and nothing in the integration proves email ownership.
- **Fix (`app/Http/Controllers/Auth/SocialAuthController.php`):**
  - `providerVerifiedEmail()`: true only for Google with `email_verified === true`. This **supersedes SEC-01's "missing claim still links" and "Facebook unaffected" decisions**; the two tests encoding them were updated, not deleted.
  - Existing account matched by email:
    - If the provider doesn't vouch, nothing is linked. Google keeps its existing message. Facebook gets "log in with your password or Google, then connect Facebook from your account settings".
    - If it vouches and the account is verified, it links as before; password, sessions and `has_password` are untouched.
    - If it vouches and the account is **unverified**, `claimUnverifiedAccount()` runs under a row lock in the existing transaction: email marked verified; password replaced with a random one and `has_password=false`, the same state as social-created accounts, so the owner can set one in Settings › Connections; `remember_token` cycled; social links attached while unverified deleted; pending OTP cleared; `referred_by_id` cleared only if the account has no orders, trashed ones included, which keeps `ReferralService`'s write-once assumption since an unverified account can't check out.
    - **Session invalidation:** every customer route has `auth.session` (Laravel's `AuthenticateSession`), which compares the session's stored password hash and the remember cookie's hash against the current password. Replacing the password logs out every other session and remember-me login on its next request. No file-session enumeration is needed (the session driver is `file`).
  - New account: verified only if the provider vouches. Otherwise it's created unverified, the 6-digit code is sent (`sendEmailVerificationNotification()`), and the user is redirected to `verification.notice`, the same destination as `LoginResponse`/`RegisterResponse`. The registration bonus and referral capture are unchanged.
  - Already-linked identities (`social_accounts` match) log in exactly as before.
  - `connectToCurrentUser()` refuses an unverified account: "Verify your email address before connecting …".
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/SocialAuthController.php`, `tests/Feature/Auth/SocialLoginTest.php` (2 tests rewritten for the new rule, 1 fixture given `email_verified: true`), `tests/Feature/Auth/SocialAccountClaimTest.php` (new).
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):**
  - `SocialAccountClaimTest` 9/9:
    - A verified account links to verified Google and keeps its password.
    - A pre-registered unverified account is claimed: verified, password changed, `has_password=false`, remember token changed, referrer cleared, the planted Facebook link removed, Google linked. The registrant's real session, captured before the claim and replayed after it, is logged out, and the old password is refused at `/login`.
    - Unverified account plus Google claim false or missing is not linked and untouched.
    - Facebook never links or claims an unverified account.
    - A new verified Google account is created verified.
    - A new Facebook account is unverified, gets the code mail and is sent to `verification.notice`.
