- **Not claimed:** that production's actual `.env`/served cookies have been inspected, or that this change has been verified against them.
- **Live production verification performed 2026-11-03 (part of the follow-up "FSA-09/FSA-11/MED-18" task) — Step B done with real evidence, Step A still blocked:**
  - **Live HTTP evidence (the actual gold-standard check, now done):** `curl -sS -D - -o /dev/null https://shoppydrive.com/` against the real production domain. The response set five cookies, including the real Laravel session cookie (`shoppydrive-session`) and `XSRF-TOKEN`. Every cookie in the response carried `secure`; every cookie except `XSRF-TOKEN` (which Laravel always sets non-`httponly` by design, so the JS-side CSRF header can read it) carried `httponly`; every cookie carried `samesite=lax`. **So right now, in production, today, the session cookie already has `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, and `SameSite=Lax` — independent of whether this local branch's code fix has been pushed yet.** Cookie values are deliberately not reproduced here, per the no-exposure instruction — only attribute presence/absence was recorded.
  - **Why this doesn't settle Step A:** this one piece of evidence is consistent with *either* of two different causes, and external HTTP alone can't distinguish them: (a) production's real `.env` already sets `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` to a truthy value explicitly, or (b) `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE` is unset there too, and Symfony's cookie layer auto-detects `secure` from the live request's actual HTTPS scheme when the config value is literally `null` — exactly the behavior this tracker's own older MED-18 entry (historical section, below) already flagged: "Symfony's Secure-cookie auto-detection already correctly reflects the real request scheme in production regardless of this env var's literal value." Both explanations produce identical, currently-observed behavior over HTTPS. This local code fix (defaulting to `true` in `config('app.env') === 'production'`) is a safety net that holds even in a scenario where the auto-detection fallback *wouldn't* apply (e.g. behind a misconfigured proxy that makes Laravel see the request as plain HTTP) — it doesn't contradict or depend on either explanation being the real one.
  - **Step A (SSH into production to read `SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE`'s actual configured value) — still blocked, exact blocker:** no SSH credentials or access to the real Hostinger production server exist in this workspace or environment; `deploy.yml`'s SSH credentials are GitHub Actions repository secrets, never exposed to a local session. No attempt was made to find, extract, or guess any such credential. **Exact safe command for the owner to run themselves** (reads one line only, never the whole `.env`, never prints a value that could be a secret since this one isn't): `ssh <user>@<host> -p <port> "grep -E '^SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE=' <deploy_path>/.env || echo '(not set — relies on Symfony auto-detection or this new code default)'"`.
  - **Conclusion:** Step B (live cookie evidence) is now genuinely verified, not assumed. Step A (the explicit production `.env` value) remains unverified — the exact command above is ready for the owner to run.

## FSA-11 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(orders): prevent admin cancellation of shipped orders (FSA-11)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `Admin\OrderController::UNCANCELLABLE_STATUSES` was `[cancelled, refunded, delivered]`, so `shipped` was cancellable once no courier submission was unresolved. Only courier status sync (`CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync()`: Pathao `in_transit`, `at_delivery_hub`, … → `shipped`) ever sets `shipped`, so the parcel is physically with the courier.
  - Cancelling restored stock still in transit, plus coupon and points through `Order::booted()`.
  - The courier's later "delivered" was then ignored as terminal, recorded but not applied, so COD could be collected against a cancelled order.
- **Existing authoritative workflow (no new workflow added):**
  - Courier cancel and return reports are mapped to `cancelled`: Pathao `pickup_cancelled`, `return_*`, `returned`, `paid_return`, … and Steadfast `cancelled_parcells`. They arrive through the HMAC-verified Steadfast webhook, the `pathao:sync-statuses` poll, or the admin "Refresh status" buttons.
  - That transition runs the same idempotent reversals (stock, coupon, points) exactly once.
  - The courier-submission reconciliation actions (`confirmReturnedConsignment`, `confirmNotCreated`) only resolve booking attempts and never change order status.
- **Fix:**
  - `Admin\OrderController::cancel()` refuses `shipped` inside its existing locked transaction, after the uncancellable check and before any write, with `SHIPPED_CANCEL_MESSAGE` telling the admin to cancel or request a return in the courier's portal. Nothing is written: no status, reason, history, mail, stock, coupon, points or payment change.
  - `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx` shows that procedure instead of the cancel form for shipped orders; the server rule is the enforcement.
  - Pending and processing cancellation, the delivered/refunded/cancelled refusals, and the unresolved-submission guard are unchanged.
- **Late courier reports:** unchanged, already safe. Once an order is `cancelled`, a later delivered report leaves status, payment, `delivered_at`, stock, coupon and points untouched and records a history row ("order is already cancelled; status left unchanged") plus an `OBSERVATION` evidence row. Covered by a new test.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderController.php`, `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx`, `tests/Feature/ShippedOrderCancellationTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/Admin/CancelDeliveredOrderTest.php` (the test "admin can still cancel a shipped order" rewritten to expect refusal, because FSA-11 replaces that rule).
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):** `ShippedOrderCancellationTest` 8/8:
  - Shipped cancel is refused for unpaid COD and for paid orders. Status and payment status are unchanged, no `stock_restored_at` or `coupon_released_at`, no reason, history or cancellation mail, and stock, points, coupon `used_count`, `CouponUse` and refund ledger rows are unchanged.
  - Pending and processing cancellation still reverses stock (+2), points (+60), coupon and the refund ledger exactly once.
  - Delivered is still refused.
  - An unresolved courier submission still blocks.
  - A Pathao return report cancels a shipped order and reverses everything once.
  - A late Pathao "Delivery" after a courier cancellation changes nothing and is recorded.
- **Sweeps:**
  - Order, courier, refund, inventory, stock and coupon sweep (all non-race files referencing them): 657 tests, 656 passed before the old shipped-cancel test was updated, then all green in the full run.
  - Full suite: 1722 tests, 1659 passed, 63 skipped (MySQL-only), 0 failed. Pint, Prettier, ESLint and `tsc` clean.
  - MySQL race files cancel only `processing` orders, so they are unaffected. MySQL tests were not run.
- **Remaining concerns:**
  - **Lost parcels (decision needed):** a parcel the courier loses may never produce a mapped cancel or return status, and the order then stays `shipped` with no admin way out. That needs a business rule, for example a mother-admin "courier confirmed lost" action with evidence and restock policy. Not invented here.
  - Orders with a consignment booked but not yet picked up (`pending`/`processing`) are still admin-cancellable, as before. The admin must also cancel at the courier, otherwise a later pickup and delivery is recorded but ignored.
- **Lost-parcel visibility follow-up, implemented 2026-11-03 (local, uncommitted, part of the consolidated 5-issue remediation batch) — closes the visibility half of the "decision needed" concern above, not the business rule itself:**
  - **Investigated first, as required:** read both `PATHAO_MAP` and `STEADFAST_MAP` in full. Neither includes a distinct "lost" raw status — Steadfast's map is explicitly "the complete set of statuses Steadfast reports, as confirmed directly by Steadfast," and Pathao's has no lost/missing entry either. So there is no specific transition to map, and inventing one would mean guessing at a courier API value that doesn't exist in this integration.
  - **What `sync()` already does safely, confirmed by reading, not assumed:** any unrecognized raw status is still recorded (`CourierDeliveryEvidence`, `OrderStatusHistory`, the raw status column) in the same transaction, but `$statusChanged` stays `false` for a `null` mapping, so the order's own status, payment status, stock and coupon/points are never touched. This already satisfies "never treat a lost parcel as delivered," "no automatic refund," "no automatic stock restore," "preserve evidence," and "prevent duplicate effects" (the existing unchanged-raw-status no-op and the row lock already cover that) — none of those needed new code.
  - **The one real gap: visibility.** Nothing surfaced "this order's courier report is unrecognized" to an admin distinctly from routine history. Added `CourierOrderStatusMapper::isUnrecognized()` and `::isTerminal()` (the latter exposing the existing private `TERMINAL_STATUSES` list to callers outside the class, reusing one definition instead of duplicating it) and a new `Admin\OrderController::possibleLostParcel()` check: true only when the order is **not** already in a terminal status (cancelled/refunded/delivered) **and** its latest stored Pathao or Steadfast raw status doesn't map to anything recognized. Surfaced as `order.needsCourierInvestigation` and an amber warning banner on the admin order page, telling the admin to contact the courier and resolve manually through the existing refund/return workflows — it performs no action itself.
  - **Customer cancellation already can't bypass this:** confirmed `routes/account.php`'s customer cancel (FSA-15) already refuses whenever `pathao_consignment_id` or `steadfast_consignment_id` is set at all, which a parcel mid-transit (lost or not) always has — no change needed there.
  - **Files:** `app/Services/CourierOrderStatusMapper.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderController.php`, `resources/js/pages/admin/orders/show.tsx`, `tests/Feature/Admin/LostParcelInvestigationTest.php` (new).
  - **Tests:** `LostParcelInvestigationTest` 6/6 — an active order with an unrecognized Pathao status is flagged; same for Steadfast; a recognized status (`in_transit`) is not flagged; an order with no courier status at all is not flagged; a cancelled order with a stale unrecognized status is not flagged (terminal); a delivered order with a stale unrecognized status is not flagged (terminal).
  - **Deliberately not done, per scope:** no automatic refund, stock restore, status change or order closure of any kind; no new courier status mapping invented; no change to `CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync()`'s own logic (it was already correct); no admin-facing index-page filter added (kept to the order detail page, where "investigate" actually happens).
  - **Outstanding owner decision (unchanged — this follow-up addresses visibility only):** the actual lost-parcel accounting/closure policy — whether a confirmed-lost parcel gets a mother-admin "confirmed lost" action, an automatic or manual restock, and/or a refund path, as the original finding's own "Lost parcels (decision needed)" note already said. This follow-up makes the condition impossible to miss; it doesn't decide what to do about it.
- **Accounting/closure policy investigation, 2026-11-03 (local, uncommitted, same pass as the FSA-09/MED-18 follow-up) — answers most of the 8 open questions from existing mechanisms, finds one genuine gap, implements no new code:**
  - **1. When is a parcel "confirmed lost"?** Never automatically. The existing `needsCourierInvestigation` flag (above) is the trigger for a human to look, not a determination by itself. A parcel is confirmed lost only when a mother admin records that determination through the **already-existing** `OrderDeliveryVerification` mechanism (`orders.deliveryVerifications.store`), using its existing `OUTCOME_NOT_DELIVERED` outcome — read in full in `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility()`: for an undelivered order, it already requires *every* linked consignment to be verified as `OUTCOME_NOT_DELIVERED` before the order becomes refund-eligible. "Lost" is simply one real-world reason behind a "not delivered" determination — it does not need its own status, column, or admin action; the mechanism that already exists for this is already the right one.
  - **2. What evidence/confirmation is required?** Whatever the mother admin already has to supply for any `OrderDeliveryVerification` today (the existing UI/flow is unchanged) — e.g. the courier's own confirmation of loss, recorded as part of that existing verification action. No new evidence type or field was added.
  - **3. COD orders:** no payment was ever collected (`payment_status` stays `unpaid`), so there is nothing to refund — the correct closure is cancellation, not a refund action. **4. Prepaid orders:** once the consignment(s) are verified `OUTCOME_NOT_DELIVERED` and a current courier-readiness attestation exists, `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility()` **already** returns `eligible: true` for the existing "Mark as Refunded" action (`RefundService::recordOrderRefund()`) — confirmed by reading its full logic, not assumed. **No new refund path or mechanism is needed for either case** — both route through flows that already exist and are already approved (PAY-RETURN-001).
  - **5. Stock restoration — the one genuine gap found, not invented:** read `Order::booted()`'s `updated` hook in full. It calls `InventoryService::restoreForOrder()` **unconditionally** on *any* transition to `CANCELLED` or `REFUNDED` — there is no existing signal anywhere that distinguishes "the physical goods came back to the merchant" (a normal return/cancellation, where restocking is correct) from "the goods are gone and will never come back" (a confirmed-lost parcel, where restocking would silently inflate stock for inventory that no longer exists). This is not a new bug introduced by this finding — it is a pre-existing, blanket behavior of the refund/cancellation side effect that a lost-parcel policy would collide with if the existing cancel/refund actions were used as-is for a lost parcel. **This is the one point in the full policy that is genuinely ambiguous and needs an owner decision** — see below.
  - **6. Coupon and loyalty-point reversal:** already correct and already automatic for both the COD-cancel and prepaid-refund paths — the same `Order::booted()` hook that (over-)restocks also calls `LoyaltyPointsService::handleOrderCancelledOrRefunded()` on the same transition, and `CouponService::release()` already runs from that same existing cancellation path. Nothing new needed here; this part of the existing mechanism is correct for a lost parcel exactly as it is for a normal one.
  - **7. Duplicate courier events / repeated admin actions:** already handled by existing, already-tested mechanisms — `CourierOrderStatusMapper::sync()`'s own row lock and unchanged-raw-status no-op prevent duplicate evidence/history from a repeated webhook; `RefundService`'s existing idempotent refund-amount-against-remaining-balance check prevents a double refund; `InventoryService::restoreForOrder()`'s `stock_restored_at` guard prevents a double restock. No new idempotency work is needed — reusing the existing refund/cancel paths inherits all of this for free.
  - **8. How the decision is recorded:** already covered — `OrderDeliveryVerification` rows and `CourierDeliveryEvidence` are both already append-only audit trails (the architecture doc's own description), and the existing cancellation/refund action already writes an `OrderStatusHistory` row. No new audit mechanism needed.
  - **Conservative-default check, item by item:** "ambiguous/unrecognized status must not auto-trigger a refund" — already true (`needsCourierInvestigation` is informational only). "Investigation state first" — already true (same flag). "Refund requires confirmed loss + authorized admin action through the existing refund process" — already true and already enforced by `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility()`'s existing not-delivered-verification requirement. "Stock not automatically restored merely because reported lost" — **this is the one point current behavior does not satisfy**, per item 5 above. "Idempotent and auditable" — already true, per items 7–8.
  - **Owner decision required (the one real gap, not resolved here):** should marking a parcel lost (via the existing refund/cancel action) skip the automatic stock restoration that currently fires unconditionally on every `CANCELLED`/`REFUNDED` transition? Two safe options, neither implemented: **(a)** accept current behavior and require the admin to manually re-decrement stock as a separate, deliberate correction after a lost-parcel refund/cancel (zero code change, relies on admin discipline); **(b)** add an explicit, opt-in way to skip the restock step for this one case specifically (e.g. a checkbox/flag on the existing refund/cancel action, read by `Order::booted()`'s hook) — a real code change to a shared mechanism used by every other refund/cancellation too, so it needs explicit sign-off before touching it, not a decision to infer. **Recommendation:** option (b) is more correct and more auditable (no reliance on an admin remembering a manual step), but touches shared financial/inventory code used by every refund in the system, so it is deliberately left as a decision rather than implemented in this pass.
  - **No new application code was written for this investigation** — it answers the policy from reading `CourierEvidenceChain`, `RefundService`, `Order::booted()`, `InventoryService`, `LoyaltyPointsService`, and `CouponService` as they already exist, confirms most of the policy needs zero new code, and isolates the one point that does to an explicit, unimplemented decision — exactly as instructed for an ambiguous financial decision.
- **Skip-restock mechanism implemented, 2026-11-03 (owner-approved, same pass) — resolves the one gap identified above:**
  - **New column:** `orders.lost_confirmed_at` (nullable timestamp, migration `2026_11_03_190000`) — null for every ordinary order; set only by the new action below. Deliberately a dedicated column, not an overload of `stock_restored_at`'s existing meaning, so the audit trail stays honest (the field name says what actually happened).
  - **`InventoryService::restoreForOrder()`:** one extra OR-condition in its existing idempotency guard — `|| $locked->lost_confirmed_at !== null` — added to the existing `if ($locked === null || $locked->stock_restored_at !== null)` early-return. This is the entire change to this shared method; every other order's cancel/refund restocking is byte-for-byte unaffected, confirmed by test (below).
  - **New `App\Services\OrderLostParcelService::confirm()`:** mother-admin only (`$admin->is_mother`, `MotherPasswordGate` — same lock/verify pattern as `OrderDeliveryVerificationService`); requires a ≥10-character note (same convention as delivery verification's own note requirement); under a row lock, refuses if already confirmed lost (idempotent — no duplicate history, no duplicate effect of any kind); requires `CourierEvidenceChain::eligibility($order)` to already report `courier_involved: true` and `eligible: true` — i.e. **exactly** the same evidence a refund already requires (every linked consignment verified `not_delivered`) — an unrecognized/ambiguous status alone is **not** accepted, satisfying that requirement directly by reusing the existing check rather than writing a new one. On success: sets `lost_confirmed_at`, writes one `OrderStatusHistory` note naming the admin and the note text. Never changes order `status` or `payment_status` itself — confirming lost is not a closure action; the admin performs the existing cancel/refund action separately afterward. Deliberately writes no `CourierDeliveryEvidence` row — that table's writers are a closed, arch-tested allowlist (`tests/Unit/CourierDeliveryEvidenceArchTest.php`) this service correctly isn't part of; the `OrderStatusHistory` note alone already satisfies "how the decision is recorded in order history."
  - **New route:** `POST admin/orders/{order}/confirm-lost` (`orders.confirmLostParcel`), `admin.mother` + `throttle:30,15` + 10-attempt session block — the identical middleware stack `orders.deliveryVerifications.store` already uses for the same class of sensitive, mother-only action.
  - **Admin order page:** `Admin\OrderController::confirmLostParcel()` thin controller wrapper (validate `password`/`note`, delegate, catch `CourierVerificationRefusedException` the same way `markRefunded()` already catches `CouldNotRecordRefundException`). `lost_confirmed_at` is automatically present in the existing `order.toArray()` payload (a real column now) — no frontend UI trigger was built in this pass (see limitation below).
  - **Files:** `database/migrations/2026_11_03_190000_add_lost_confirmed_at_to_orders_table.php` (new), `app/Models/Order.php`, `app/Services/InventoryService.php`, `app/Services/OrderLostParcelService.php` (new), `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrderController.php`, `routes/admin.php`, `tests/Feature/Admin/LostParcelConfirmationTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/Admin/AdminRoutePermissionWiringTest.php` (one exemption-list entry added during the final pre-commit review — see below).
  - **Tests:** `LostParcelConfirmationTest` 11/11 — route middleware (mother/throttle/block/web/admin) correct; an ordinary admin is refused and a guest redirected; a wrong password is refused; an unrecognized status with **no** existing not-delivered verification is refused (directly proving "an unrecognized status alone is not sufficient proof of loss"); an order with no courier involvement at all is refused; a mother admin confirms a parcel lost once existing evidence already shows not-delivered, with status/payment left untouched and a history row written; confirming an already-confirmed order again is refused with no duplicate history row (idempotency); **a confirmed-lost COD parcel is cancelled without restoring stock** (requirement 1); **a confirmed-lost prepaid parcel is refunded through the existing, unchanged "Mark as Refunded" action without restoring stock** (requirement 1, prepaid variant — `OrderRefund` ledger row confirms the refund was genuinely recorded, since a whole-order refund correctly leaves `payment_status` at `paid` rather than `refunded` when shipping is excluded, an existing, unrelated PAY-RETURN-001 behavior, not a new bug); **ordinary cancellation of an order never confirmed lost still restores stock exactly as before** (requirement 2); **ordinary refund of an order never confirmed lost still restores stock exactly as before** (requirement 3). Requirement 4 (no duplicate financial/inventory effects) is covered by the idempotency test above plus `restoreForOrder()`'s own pre-existing, untouched `stock_restored_at` guard, which still independently prevents a double-restock regardless of `lost_confirmed_at`.
  - **Regression:** full order/refund/inventory/delivery-verification sweep (`LostParcelInvestigationTest`, `LostParcelConfirmationTest`, `ShippedOrderCancellationTest`, `OrderRefundTest`, `CustomerOrderCancellationTest`, `InventoryEnforcementTest`, `OrderHistoryProtectionTest`, `Admin\DeliveryVerificationTest`): 137/137. Pint clean.
  - **Known limitation found while testing, not silently glossed over:** a genuinely **`shipped`** order's parcel, once confirmed lost, still cannot be closed through the admin-cancel action — `Admin\OrderController::cancel()` refuses any `shipped` order outright, by FSA-11's own original, separate, intentional rule (the courier's own report is supposed to be the only path for a shipped order). This mechanism's COD-closure test therefore necessarily uses a `processing` order (courier-involved via a linked consignment, but not yet `shipped` in this app's own status field) to demonstrate the skip-restock behavior — a real `shipped`-and-lost COD order currently has **no** closure path at all, confirmed-lost or not. This is a pre-existing gap this task did not introduce and was not asked to resolve; it would need its own explicit decision (e.g. a mother-admin-only override of the shipped-cancel refusal, specifically for a confirmed-lost parcel) if the owner wants it closed.
  - **Not done, per explicit instruction:** no change to normal cancellation/refund restocking behavior for any order other than one explicitly confirmed lost; no automatic refund of a COD order (COD closure still goes through the ordinary, unchanged cancel action, which happens to collect no money so there is nothing to refund); no new financial ledger entries invented anywhere.
  - **One real regression caught and fixed during the 2026-11-04 final pre-commit review:** `tests/Feature/Admin/AdminRoutePermissionWiringTest.php` (LOW-05's own architecture test — every `admin.*` route must carry exactly one `admin.can:` middleware entry unless explicitly exempted with a verified reason) correctly failed because the new `admin.orders.confirmLostParcel` route — mother-admin-only via `['admin.mother', 'throttle:30,15']`, the identical pattern already used by `admin.orders.deliveryVerifications.store` — had not been added to that file's `LOW05_EXEMPT_ADMIN_ROUTES` list. Fixed by adding the one documented exemption entry, matching the existing `deliveryVerifications.store` entry's reasoning exactly. Full suite re-run green afterward (see below). No other defect was found.

## FSA-12 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(checkout): secure order confirmation and preserve bundle attribution (FSA-03, FSA-12)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Trace:**
  - The checkout UI submits no `bundle_id` (`Checkout.tsx`'s items carry only product, variant and quantity).
  - Bundle tags on the cart are decided server-side in `CartManager::revalidatePrices()`, which runs on every checkout before both COD and mobile-banking staging. It calls `BundleService::reconcile()`, which strips tags whose group isn't an intact, active bundle, then `autoAssemble()`, which tags complete recipes.
  - `CheckoutController::initiateMobileBanking()` copies each cart item's resulting `bundle_id` into the server-side `PendingCheckout.checkout_data` snapshot, which the client can't modify.
  - COD (`CheckoutController.php`, order item creation) and legacy ZiniPay (`OrderService.php:97`) persist it.
- **Root cause:** `MobilePaymentController::submit()` built each `OrderItem` from the snapshot but omitted `bundle_id`, so every paid mobile-banking order lost its bundle attribution. The charged bundle-prorated prices themselves were correct.
- **Fix:** add `'bundle_id' => $item['bundle_id'] ?? null` to that `OrderItem` create, the same pattern as `OrderService`. A snapshot without the key (an ordinary item or an older row) gives `null`. No new trust in client input: the value is the server-reconciled staged tag. The landing-page mobile flow has no bundles and is unchanged.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/MobilePaymentController.php` (one line), `tests/Feature/MobileBankingBundleAttributionTest.php` (new).
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite):** `MobileBankingBundleAttributionTest` 3/3 (19 assertions), each staging through `checkout.store` and paying with a matched SMS:
  - An intact bundle's two items keep `bundle_id` at their prorated 90/30 prices, and an ordinary item stays `null`.
  - Two different bundles in one order keep their own ids.
  - A bundle id the submitted product isn't part of is stripped before staging, so the item is `null` at full price.
- COD attribution is still covered unchanged by `BundleCheckoutTest`, and the partial-group "price bounce" is already covered there. Payment verification is unchanged (`MobilePaymentMatchingTest` in the sweep).
- **Sweeps:** mobile, bundle, checkout, landing, payment, loyalty, coupon and confirmation files: 177 tests, 164 passed, 13 skipped (MySQL-only). Full suite: 1725 tests, 1662 passed, 63 skipped, 0 failed. Pint clean. No MySQL race file references `bundle_id`. MySQL tests were not run.
- **Commit note:** `MobilePaymentController.php` also carries FSA-03's confirmation grants. FSA-12's line can go into the FSA-03 commit, or the file can be split with a patch.
- **Remaining:** past mobile-banking orders already lack `bundle_id`. Restoring it would mean inferring from prices or snapshots, a separate data-repair decision. Not done.

## FSA-13 notes

**Status: DEPLOYED_UNVERIFIED, 2026-11-03: committed in "fix(newsletter): queue newsletter delivery in subscriber chunks (FSA-13)", deployed by run `41575932713` (push `0ef9d9a..17a1501`). Functional production verification pending.**

- **Root cause:** `Admin\NewsletterController::send()` built one message with every active subscriber in BCC and sent it synchronously inside the admin's request (`->send()` on a non-queued Mailable).
  - Shared-hosting SMTP per-message recipient caps or PHP's time limit failed the whole send.
  - A failure left no record of who had or hadn't received it.
  - Uploaded attachments were stored in the shared `newsletter-attachments/tmp` and never deleted.
- **Queue architecture (existing infrastructure only):**
  - The `database` queue drained by the scheduled `app:drain-queue` (one `queue:work --once` process per job, 45s timeout, `--tries=3`), plus Laravel job batches.
  - `job_batches` already exists in `0001_01_01_000002_create_jobs_table`, so there is no migration and no new service.
- **Implementation:**
  - `send()` now validates as before, collects active subscribers ordered by id, de-duplicated case-insensitively (keeping the stored address), and splits them into chunks of `RECIPIENTS_PER_MESSAGE = 50`. It then dispatches one `Bus::batch` named `newsletter:{sendId}` of `App\Jobs\SendNewsletterChunk` jobs with `allowFailures()`, so one bad chunk doesn't cancel the rest.
  - The admin sees "queued for N subscribers in M message(s)". Authorization (`admin.can:newsletter,create`), validation and HTML purification are unchanged.
  - `SendNewsletterChunk` (`Batchable`, `Queueable`, `$tries = 3`, `$backoff = 60`):
    - Skips if the batch is cancelled.
    - Re-reads which of its emails are **still active**, so unsubscribes during sending are respected.
    - Fails, and stays retryable, if any attachment is missing or a promoted product was deleted, rather than sending a degraded newsletter.
    - Sends **one** BCC message as its last step. A failed attempt sends nothing, so a retry repeats only an undelivered message and never touches another chunk. The only duplicate window is the SMTP server accepting a message and the connection then failing before the job completes, which is inherent to SMTP.
  - Content is unchanged: the same `NewsletterCustomMail` / `NewsletterProductMail`. The product mail now loads the product inside the job.
- **Attachment lifecycle:**
  - Uploads are stored in `newsletter-attachments/{sendId}/` on the local disk.
  - The batch's `then` callback deletes that directory. Laravel runs `then` only when the batch's pending count reaches 0; a permanently failed job does not decrement it, but a failed job that is later retried and succeeds does. So the files are deleted only after **every** chunk has been delivered, never while a failed chunk could still be retried.
  - Backstop: new `newsletter:prune-attachments` (`PruneNewsletterAttachmentsCommand`), scheduled daily at 03:00 Asia/Dhaka with `withoutOverlapping(1440, false)`, like the other daily commands. It removes a send's directory when its batch is finished (the callback failed), when no batch exists after 24 hours (the request died before dispatch), or after 30 days unfinished (abandoned failures; a later retry then fails on the missing-attachment check instead of sending without it). It also clears legacy `tmp/` files older than 24 hours. A batch still in progress is never touched.
- **Failure visibility:** a chunk that exhausts its 3 tries lands in `failed_jobs` and is listed in Admin › Failed jobs (MED-03). That page shows only the class name and a redacted error line, never the payload with its emails. `SendNewsletterChunk` is **not** in `FailedJobSummary::RETRYABLE_JOBS`, so retrying it currently needs `php artisan queue:retry`. Adding it would change MED-03 code and is left as a decision; the job meets that list's stated criteria.
- **Files:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/NewsletterController.php`, `app/Jobs/SendNewsletterChunk.php` (new), `app/Console/Commands/PruneNewsletterAttachmentsCommand.php` (new), `routes/console.php`, `tests/Feature/Admin/NewsletterSendTest.php` (7 tests added; the 2 sanitizer tests unchanged), `tests/Feature/Admin/PruneNewsletterAttachmentsCommandTest.php` (new), `tests/Feature/QueueWorkerScheduleTest.php` (expects the 6th scheduled event).
- **Tests (in-memory SQLite; no real mail: `Mail::fake()` or the `array` mailer):**
  - `NewsletterSendTest` 9/9:
    - The request queues one batch of `[50, 50, 21]` chunk jobs with `allowFailures` and sends nothing synchronously.
    - 121 unique active subscribers plus one case-variant duplicate and one inactive give 3 messages of ≤50, each person exactly once, the inactive one excluded.
    - Subject, body and attachment reach every chunk, and the upload is deleted after all are delivered.
    - Failure lifecycle, on the real `database` queue with an in-process worker and a send-time listener that rejects one chunk: chunk 1 delivered once, the bad chunk tried 3 times (60s backoff, clock advanced) and then in `failed_jobs`, attachments still present; `queue:retry` with the fault cleared gives 2 messages total, all 60 recipients exactly once, and the attachments deleted.
    - An unsubscribe after queuing is respected.
    - An admin with only `newsletter.view` gets 403 and nothing is batched.
    - The 2 existing purification tests pass unchanged.
  - `PruneNewsletterAttachmentsCommandTest` 5/5: finished batch removed; in-progress batch with a failed chunk kept at 10 days; abandoned removed at 31 days; no-batch upload removed after a day and kept before; legacy `tmp` old removed and fresh kept.
  - Focused newsletter and schedule files: 24/24.
  - Full suite: 1736 tests, 1673 passed, 63 skipped, 0 failed. Pint clean.
  - The first full run failed one new test only: the in-test worker hit `queue:work`'s default 128 MB memory stop late in the suite. The test helper now passes `--memory=4096`, and the suite is green.
  - No MySQL race file touches newsletters or batches. MySQL and real SMTP were not exercised.
- **Remaining limitations:**
  - **Throughput:** `app:drain-queue` runs about 50s per minute, one job per process. A 10,000-subscriber list is 200 jobs, so delivery spreads over a few minutes, slower if the SMTP host throttles. Not measured on Hostinger.
  - The 50-recipient chunk is a conservative assumption about Hostinger's per-message cap, not a verified figure.
  - Newsletter sends are not written to the admin audit log (unchanged; not part of FSA-13).
  - The success message no longer says "sent", because delivery is now asynchronous.
- **Final review (2026-11-03, no code changed):**
  - The diff is confined to FSA-13 files: `NewsletterController`, the new job, the new command, `routes/console.php`, `NewsletterSendTest`, the new prune test, `QueueWorkerScheduleTest`, and this tracker. No other finding touches them.
  - Batch semantics, re-read in the installed framework:
    - `Batch::recordSuccessfulJob()` runs `then` only when `pendingJobs === 0`.
    - `recordFailedJob()` only increments the failed count, so a dead chunk keeps the batch open.
    - Callbacks run inside `invokeHandlerCallback()`'s try/catch, so a failing cleanup is only reported and can never fail or retry the chunk that triggered it. The local disk is also `'throw' => false`.
  - `app:drain-queue` processes connection `database`, queue `default`, which is where `Bus::batch()` dispatches with `QUEUE_CONNECTION=database`, the same assumption as every existing queued mail. `then` runs inside that job's `--once` child process.
  - The prune command can't touch a batch younger than 30 days that hasn't finished.
  - The schedule is `0 3 * * *` with timezone Asia/Dhaka (21:00 UTC), and the prune command's age maths uses Unix timestamps, so it is timezone-independent.
  - Focused files re-run: 24/24.
- **Residual risks recorded by the review:**
  1. **Post-send window:** the mail is sent before the job reports success. If the SMTP server accepts the message and then the connection drops, or the batch bookkeeping fails, or `app:drain-queue` kills the process at its 45s timeout after acceptance, the job is retried and that one chunk (≤50 people) gets a second copy. This is inherent to SMTP plus at-least-once queues and can't be closed without an SMTP-side idempotency key. Normal failures (refusal, timeout before acceptance, missing attachment, deleted product) send nothing.
  2. **Large messages vs the 45s per-job timeout:** `attachments.*` is capped at 10 MB each with no count limit (unchanged validation). A very large message may not finish uploading within 45s; it then fails after 3 tries (visible in Failed jobs) rather than sending. A total-size cap would be a separate decision.
  3. **Double submit:** two submissions of the compose form create two batches, so subscribers get two newsletters, as with the old synchronous code. "One copy per subscriber" holds per send operation; cross-submission idempotency was not in scope.
  4. **`APP_KEY` rotation** while a batch is in flight makes its stored `then` closure unverifiable. The chunks then fail closed (nothing sent), and the prune command removes the files after 30 days.
- **Deployment-workflow follow-up fixed 2026-11-03 (local, uncommitted, part of the consolidated 5-issue remediation batch — not a code change to anything above):** `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`'s rsync step (`--delete`, no releases/symlink/activation step — files are live as they land) had no exclusion for `storage/app/private`, where FSA-13's in-flight newsletter attachments live only on the server, never in the repo being synced — so any deploy during an in-flight send would have deleted them, failing every not-yet-delivered chunk closed (same failure mode `PruneNewsletterAttachmentsCommand` already handles, just triggered by a deploy instead of time). Fixed by adding `--exclude='storage/app/private/*'` to the existing exclude list, alongside the pre-existing `storage/app/public/*` exclusion — not a broad `storage/*` exclusion, so every other deploy-managed storage path (`framework/cache/data`, `framework/sessions`, `framework/views`, `logs`) is unaffected. `--exclude` alone (no `--delete-excluded`) already protects an excluded path from `--delete` by rsync's own semantics — confirmed by reading rsync's documented behavior, not assumed. New `tests/Feature/DeployWorkflowStorageExclusionTest.php` (4/4): the workflow file is valid YAML; the new exclusion is present; every pre-existing exclusion is still present; no broad `storage/*`/`storage` exclusion was introduced. No deployment secrets, SSH credentials, server configuration or production files were touched. Not deployed — this only takes effect on the next real deploy.

| MED-09 | Medium | Money cast to float across models | COMPLETED (LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION). Deployment: NOT DEPLOYED | **Root cause confirmed, one concrete site fixed, no broad retrofit performed (deliberately, per the report's own recommendation).** `Order`/`OrderItem`/`CartItem`/`Product`/`ProductVariant`/`Payment` all cast exact `decimal(10,2)` columns to PHP `'float'`, exposing any later PHP-side arithmetic/comparison to IEEE-754 noise. Searched every `!==`/`===` against a money field in `app/Http/Controllers`, `app/Support`, `app/Services`: the only genuine value comparison (not a null-check) was `CartManager::revalidatePrices()` (`app/Support/CartManager.php:462`): `round((float) $item->price, 2) !== $current`, a strict comparison of two independently-computed floats. Fixed to compare in integer poisha instead: `(int) round((float) $item->price * 100) !== (int) round($current * 100)` — the exact convention already established and reviewed at `app/Services/ManualOrderPricing.php:93-96` (`differs()`), not a new pattern. `InventoryService.php:283`'s tolerance-band comparison, `ManualOrderPricing.php`, and every other float-cast model are explicitly unchanged — no broad retrofit, matching the report's own "no broad retrofit recommended given blast radius" | `app/Support/CartManager.php`, `tests/Feature/CartPriceRevalidationTest.php` (new) | `CartPriceRevalidationTest`: 4/4 on in-memory SQLite, 10 assertions (the same effective price via a different calculation path — `199.99 * 0.85` vs a flat `169.99` — is not a false change; a genuine one-poisha change is still detected; a genuine large change is still detected; an unchanged price leaves the item untouched). With the pre-existing `CartConcurrencyTest` and `BundlePricingTest` (both already exercise `revalidatePrices()`): 28/28, 0 failed | Pint passed. **Empirically could not construct a currency-scale value pair where the OLD `round(,2)` comparison actually disagreed** (brute-forced 100,000 cent values against an alternate float path; also checked a percentage-discount-vs-flat-price pair — all agreed after rounding) — confirms the report's own "PROBABLE/THEORETICAL... no concrete failing value combination demonstrated" classification exactly. The fix's value is correctness-by-construction (exact integer equality) rather than reliance on `round()`'s internal behavior, not the resolution of an observed live failure.
